戰火影響料屬短期，無礙市場中期走勢
2026 年 4 月
美國對伊朗展開襲擊所引發的中東戰火延續至今，拖累 3 月截至 27 日的全球金融股市表現，
市場在多重變數交織下動能顯著受壓，主要股市指數單月報酬全盤皆墨。不過，在能源類股大漲的支
撐下，S&P 500 指數表現相對其他股市穩定。日本股市雖亦出現獲利了結壓力，但受惠於高市政府
「負責且積極的財政政策」 及日圓匯率弱勢有利出口 ， 今年以來 MSCI 日本指數的表現僅次於拉丁美
洲股市。另一方面，受油價高漲帶動通膨預期、外資持續撤出且經濟放緩所累，印度股市出現賣壓，

MSCI 印度指數不論在 3 月或是今年至今表現都是敬陪末座。
全球主要債市 3 月報酬亦全數下跌。在所有券種之中 ， 以新興當地債下跌 5.21% 表現最為疲弱 ，
除了油價引發市場對於進口原油的新興國家前景的擔憂外 ，高漲的美元指數也使得當地貨幣計價新興
債表現呈壓。 全球主權債同樣因為油價高漲推升通膨疑慮 ，投資人對於各國央行貨幣政策或因而轉為
更為鷹派高度警戒，使其表現差強人意。
自去年川普關稅「解放日」後美股跌至低點後開始走揚，各國股市與主要指數持續上揚，其中又
以金礦指數、南韓股市、費半指數、日本股市表現最佳。然而，自中東戰事 2/28 爆發後，以指數修
正幅度來看，跌幅最大的市場分別為金礦指數、南韓股市、日本股市。唯有費半指數則在 AI 需求暢
旺的加持下成為例外。由此可見，本波市場的回跌，基本上為「漲多跌多」的修正效應。
就基本面來看，2026 年美股企業獲利增長主軸仍在 AI， 由於2026 年仍在 AI 基礎建設期擴張間 ，
AI 終端需求仍是科技龍頭企業，故所帶動的獲利增長料不致受油價影響，今年單季盈餘增長可望維

持 2 位數水準。自中東戰事以來，彭博對於 MSCI 美國指數 Q1 及 Q2 的 EPS 預期不跌反漲，顯示市
場對於美國企業的獲利前景仍極具信心。同時，目前的資訊科技類股的預期 P/E 比與過去 10 年的均
值相較仍過有遭到低估之嫌，也預示科技類股相較於其他類股仍有進一步向上的動能。
再者，儘管近期美國公債殖利率因市場的通膨疑慮而上揚 ， 但目前殖利率遠高於Fed 長期通膨目
標，投資人仍可以資產配置角度對於債券作適當之配置。考量短期殖利率受市場對 Fed 的利率政策
預期變動影響較大，故在年期與券種的選擇上可以 7~15 年的中長期美國公債或投資級債為主。

美國股市：標普 500 企業盈餘增長加速，私募信貸贖回潮不致衝擊美國經濟
美國第四季 GDP 季增年率修正值 0.7%，較初值 1.4%有所調降，主要反映實質個人消費季比降
至 0.7%。第四季 GDP 數據整體仍支持美國經濟穩健。但第一季受通膨(關稅與伊朗危機)與高利率影
響，消費行為呈現「降級消費」特徵，非必需性支出收縮，經濟呈現倚賴「高收入族群支撐消費」的
K 型分化格局。由於中東戰火推高能源價格，美國民眾擔心生活成本壓力惡化，美國密西根大學3 月
消費者信心指數降至 55.5，較 2 月終值 56.6 下降 1.1，觸及今年以來最低紀錄，反映中東危機與生

活成本壓力削弱民眾樂觀情緒。
2 月非農就業人數淨減少 9.2 萬個，低於彭博預期的增加 1.3 萬，失業率躍升至 4.4%，失業率雖
短期上揚、但就長期角度仍在低檔。追究原因數據主要是受到罷工和天氣等短期因素干擾，導致醫療
健保、建築業、零售業和休閒酒店等產業表現不佳，但整體勞動市場狀態溫和，從請領就業金人數等
數據看來就業市場尚未出現顯著惡化跡象。市場先前擔憂 AI 取代人力，目前數據看來企業僅是招募
保守，後續可觀察勞動市場降溫是否反映在薪資成長，若薪資壓力下降有助於抑制服務業通膨，除直
接改善企業成本結構外，也將使聯準會擔憂的服務業通膨出現放緩。
預估第一季標普 500 企業餘年增仍可達到 11.3%，且在第二季顯著拉升至近兩成水準，科技板
塊受惠於 AI 仍是帶動美股企業獲利增長主軸。大型科技巨擘透過AI 優化流程，開始對利潤率產生正
面影響。然而，此一效益目前高度集中於資本與技術優勢明顯的科技龍頭企業，中小企業與非科技產
業尚未顯著受益。因此，企業獲利將呈現明顯分化格局，而尚未受惠於 AI 的非科技業者，將會在未

來幾年逐步提高使用 AI 程度，傳統企業大規模導入 AI 模型與使用算力，將可成為 AI 龍頭下一波業
績成長動能。
近期美國私募信貸市場爆發贖回潮，包含貝萊德、阿波羅、藍鷹(Blue Owl)等巨頭均遭投資人要
求提款，甚至限制贖回以防止流動性枯竭。此波動盪主因投資人擔憂 AI 技術衝擊軟體業導致違約風
險上升，以及對高收益資產的獲利了結。研判此類事件雖造成金融股拉回，但引發總體系統性危機的
可能性極低。
目前標普 500 本益比估計在 22.84 倍、股價淨值比 4.95 倍，短期評價偏高，面對伊朗戰事的干
擾，預料股市震盪加劇，但若市場拉回，基於美國在 AI 產業具備絕對優勢，仍建議以長線角度布局。

歐洲股市：歐元區經濟表現穩健，央行面對能源危機採取警戒態度
歐元區第四季 GDP 季增率自初值的 0.3%下修至 0.2%，年增則下修至 1.2%，二者皆低於市場預
期。歐元區第四季經濟動能略微放緩，成長主要由內需帶動。家庭消費在穩健的勞動力市場與薪資增
長支撐下，季增率上升至 0.2%，固定資本形成雖由高點回落，但仍維持正成長，外部需求疲軟導致
出口季減，加上企業去庫存，顯示內需支撐正與貿易逆風強烈拉鋸。
三月份歐元區經濟活動連續 15 個月處於擴張區間，其中製造業表現強勁，歐元區製造業PMI 由
50.8 上升至 51.4，創 45 個月高點，受新訂單增長支撐，惟出口訂單連續第 49 個月低於榮枯線，外

需疲軟仍未改善。服務業受中東戰爭引發的不確定性與能源成本飆升衝擊，驟降至 50.1，放緩至 10
個月低點。預料南歐以觀光業為主國家將面對能源成本衝擊，而外銷為主的如德國、法國將迎來能源
與運輸成本飆升的雙重打擊。歐盟決定延後提交原定 4/15 提出將俄羅斯石油進口永久禁令入法的提
案，自 2022 年俄羅斯全面入侵烏克蘭以來，歐盟已大幅降低對俄能源依賴，是否因為伊朗戰爭調整
制裁內容將是未來關注重點。
歐洲央行在 3 月利率決策會議決定利率不變、但態度轉鷹。歐洲央行總裁拉加德表示，若通膨出
現「顯著但不過於持久」的超標情況，可能進行「適度的政策調整」，以確保通膨預期維持穩定。她
強調 ， 若對通膨偏離目標完全不作反應 ， 恐影響政策溝通與市場信心 ， 使外界難以理解央行反應機制 。
從利率期貨觀察 ， 市場預期歐洲央行與英國央行都有機會在第二季升息1 碼 ， 不排除未來一年升息到
2 碼，顯示歐洲央行與英國央行面對能源危機採取警戒態度。
在地緣風險常態化背景下，歐洲各國持續提高國防支出，德國政府規劃在2026 年投入 380 億歐

元於基礎建設、氣候保護專項基金、國防及數位化，加上 AI 浪潮帶動企業投資，投資者原本對歐洲
經濟抱持樂觀態度，然而油價大漲大幅扭轉市場樂觀預期。 考量市場目前悲觀原因仍是地緣政治風
險，假設美伊衝突能在第二季落幕，對歐股企業獲利影響有限，歐洲經濟將在下半年重回成長軌道。
泛歐 600 指數目前本益比為 16.14，故價淨值比為 2.27。就評價角度相對合理，雖然年初以來歐
股的樂觀情緒因為伊朗戰爭快速扭轉，但未來若油價回落，或是歐洲重新考慮向俄羅斯購買能源，都
能夠化解歐洲面臨的能源困境，故仍可視為長線佈局標的。

日本股市：高市政府加大戰略產業投資，政府補貼壓抑油價衝擊
日本受惠於企業強勁支出 ，2025 年第四季 GDP 年化季增率為 1.3%， 優於原先預估的0.2%。2026
年不僅企業資本支出動能持續 ， 在高市早苗出任首相後 ， 政府選定支持包含實體人工智慧 、 再生醫學 、
量子運算與海洋無人機等 61 個項目，目標在 2040 年前取得全球 AI 機器人逾 30%的市占率，國產
晶片銷售額也要在同年衝上 40 兆日圓。高市希望藉由政府與民間的合作推動投資，強化發展包含人
工智慧、半導體、宇宙及航太等 17 大重點產業。由於自民黨已在國會選舉取得空前壓倒性勝利，產

業補貼計畫將可落實，讓政府投資不僅貢獻短期 GDP，亦可提升日本長期經濟競爭力。
日本 2 月剔除生鮮食品的消費者價格年比上漲 1.6%，創 2022 年 3 月以來最小升幅，主要因為
公用事業補貼壓低能源成本。日本勞動組合總連合會(連合)近期公布 2026 年春鬥第 1 次彙整結果，
加薪幅度雖低於去年同期 、 不過已連3 年高於 5%，且2026 年中小企業加薪幅度可望高於過去幾年，
支撐日本薪資物價形成正向循環，並達成實質薪資增長。近期伊朗戰事導致油價飆升，首相高市早苗
積極應對，除釋出戰備儲油外，將控制汽油價格上限，故預料短期通膨不致大幅躍昇。
日本央行三月利率決策會議維持政策不變，主要考量來自中東局勢不確定性，難以衡量伊朗戰爭
及國際油價飆漲對日本經濟及通膨帶來影響 ，呼應日本央行總裁植田和男論點 ， 希望有更多時間可以
觀察對物價的引導。後市央行升息時點須留意4 月初將公布的短觀報告，若企業信心尚維持正面，搭
配伊朗戰爭如白宮所預期的在 5、6 周之內結束，則日本央行或有望在第二季升息一碼。預料升息進

度放緩將使十年期日本公債殖利率在 2.0~2.5%區間震盪，且由於全球央行方向轉鷹、美元受惠於避
險需求，日本央行緩步升息有助日圓匯率穩定。
波克夏將對東京海上進行出資 ， 在4 月藉由收購東京海上持有的自家庫藏股 、 取得東京海上2.49%
股權，出資額為 2,874 億日圓。在巴菲特掌舵時期，波克夏對日本企業的投資以大型商社為主，此次
將是波克夏首度和日本金融機構進行合作 ， 性質與過去投資商社股不同 ， 若波克夏未來對日投資可擴
及其他非商社股，對於日股長期表現將帶來更大力度的加持。
2026 年以來日股受惠於高市擴大財政刺激 、強化軍事安全、鞏固美日同盟上，但本益比仍在 18.38
倍，股價淨值比在 1.68 倍。3 月下旬外資賣超日股，估計是受到伊朗戰事干擾且避免股利發放的課
稅而調整持股部位所致。近期日圓震盪走貶，有利於於日本外銷股財報表現，預料企業獲利可望為日
股在全球金融市場震盪下帶來支撐。

新興亞洲股市：中東戰事對經濟影響負面，但成長仍具韌性
亞洲股市 3 月因中東情勢緊張而大跌，截至 3/27，亞洲不含日本指數 (MXAPJ) 單月下跌
10.39%。南韓股市跌幅居前，單月下跌 12.90%，多次於盤中啟動熔斷機制，除了中東地緣政治風
險之外，漲幅過大後的技術性修正也是主因。台股在內資支撐下，跌幅相對較輕，單月下跌 6.50%。
中國股市也表現弱勢，上證指數下跌 5.99%，國企指數下跌 4.58%。3 月份正值全國兩會結束，股市
出現了「利多出盡」的調節。MSCI 東協指數 3 月下跌 3.53%，是跌幅較輕的亞洲區域。馬來西亞與

印尼身為原油、液化天然氣（LNG）及棕櫚油的重要出口國，油價上漲直接帶動其相關權值股走強，
抵銷了跌幅，但越南、菲律賓等原油進口國，跌幅超過 10%。印度 SENSEX 指數下跌 9.48%，印度
是全球第三大石油進口國，約 85% 的原油依賴進口，油價高漲對經濟造衝擊，另外外資於三月大量
賣出，也是印度股市重挫的原因。
亞洲經濟在 2026 年 Q2 將面對能源價格的劇烈波動對經濟的衝擊。根據 ADB 資料，泰國、
南韓與新加坡及印度等國，其能源進口依賴度顯著高於區域平均 。在當前中東衝突陰影未散的背景
下，油價的每一次跳動都觸發亞洲通膨的扳機，對於這些原油高依賴進口國家而言，能源成本的上升
不僅僅是物價問題，更是對 GDP 增長動能的直接侵蝕。
根據野村的推估「油價每上漲 10%」對各國經濟的衝擊路徑各不相同，印度與菲律賓的 CPI 通
膨壓力最為顯著，預計分別跳升 0.5 個百分點，這將迫使兩國央行在 Q2 維持較高利率環境，壓縮
降息空間 。反觀馬來西亞與印尼，受惠於其資源生產國的地位，GDP 增長甚至可能出現微幅的正向

回饋或較小的負面衝擊 。這種「能源非對稱性」將導致亞洲內部經濟與貨幣政策的進一步分化。
然而亞洲經濟在 2026 年 Q2 面臨增長放緩的挑戰。但根據博鰲亞洲論壇報告表示，受國際貿易不
確定性及地緣政治風險上升影響，今年亞洲增長率預計從去年的 4.7% 降至 4.5%。儘管增速微降，
但亞洲經濟體 GDP 占世界比重持續上升，依然是全球經濟的主要成長引擎。這與 ADB 資料相呼
應，顯示亞洲在全球經濟增速放緩的大環境下，憑藉區域一體化進程展現出較佳的韌性 。
對於印度與菲律賓等高通膨敏感區，應關注其內需韌性能否抵銷能源稅的負面影響；而對於新加
坡、南韓與台灣等雖然進口佔比較高但產業鏈地位穩固的經濟體，仍看好在全球 AI 供應鏈帶來的快
速增長。整體而言亞洲國家在 2026 年的經濟增長整體保持韌性，但將出現明確分化。

中國股市：轉型陣痛與外部地緣風險的夾擊
2026 年中國兩會不僅發佈經濟目標，更是「十五五規劃」首年的轉型宣言。官方將 GDP 成長
目標設定在 4.5% 至 5.0% 的區間，這項數據釋放了一個明確信號，北京已不再盲目追求過往的高
速增長，而是將重心置於「新質生產力」的質變與落地。在政策宣示中，我們觀察到由「總量擴張」
向 「結構優化」 的顯著移轉。2026 年數位經濟核心產業佔 GDP 比重目標提升至 12.5%，重點布局
人工智能（AI Agent）與具身智能（Physical AI）的工業化應用。同時，政府推動的大規模「設備更

新與消費品以舊換新」 政策，試圖在傳統房產動能消退之際，透過激發存量市場的替代需求來維持內
需熱度。這種以新經濟與高品質消費為主的政策組合，旨在對沖房地產下行對內需產生的結構性缺
口，並賦予國家經濟轉型的重任。
中國經濟正經歷一場歷史性的動能切換，長期作為增長引擎的房地產，其最終需求對 GDP 的佔
比正持續回落。與此同時，以醫藥、先進設備、IT 通訊及研發為首的「高科技 + 新三樣」產業，其
貢獻權重已經在 2025 年正式超越房地產。轉型過程雖然伴隨著陣痛，卻是中國邁向科技自主的必要
路徑 。 儘管房地產的長尾效應仍在消化 ， 但這些新興領域的快速擴張正為中國經濟建立更具韌性的基
礎，成為觀察中國能否維持區域增速預期的核心支柱。對於投資者而言，這意味著舊有的投資邏輯已
徹底改變，未來市場的超額收益將高度集中在具備全球競爭力的先進製造龍頭。
儘管新經濟轉型積極，但房地產市場的下滑壓力依然是中國最沉重的負擔。根據彭博模型，房地
產尋底之路極為漫長。預測路徑顯示，未來幾年房地產佔 GDP 比重將持續收縮至 12% 左右。中

國的政策博弈重點在於政府如何透過擴張性財政手段來進行 「止血」 ，防止房市超跌引發系統性金融
風險。但在目前市場信心修復極為緩慢的背景下，觀察銷售數據能否在超跌區間出現初步止穩。
然而中國面臨的外部環境更加嚴峻。中東危機的突然升溫，成為干擾復甦的關鍵變數。地緣震盪
推升了能源溢價，加劇輸入型通膨壓力，美國聯準會（Fed）態度的轉向鷹派與美元持續走強，顯著
壓縮了中國央行的降息空間。在強美元與高利差的雙重擠壓下，人民幣匯率面臨巨大的貶值壓力。這
種「內需待援、外部受限」的政策困局，使得操作空間顯得倍加受限，雖然兩會釋放了擴張性財政的
積極信號，但在地緣政治風險與強勢美元背景下，市場缺乏強勁的資金流入動能，也因此我們認為中
國股市欠缺全面性上漲的機會。類股建議在配置上規避傳統房地產產業，鎖定受惠於新質生產力政
策、具備技術護城河且能抵禦外部波動的科技領先標的。

印度股市： 中東戰事與 Fed 轉鷹影響印度成長動能
印度的實體經濟活動成長發出趨緩信號。3 月份最新發布的 HSBC Flash 印度綜合 PMI 指數由 2
月的 58.9 降至 56.5，創下 2022 年 10 月以來最弱的擴張速度，反映出國內需求在物價壓力下正
逐步降溫 。其中，製造業 PMI 降至 53.8，顯示國內訂單成長受阻；然而，值得關注的是國際訂單
卻創下調查歷史新高，這證明在全球供應鏈重組的戰略大環境下，印度製造的外部需求依然堅挺 。
儘管基本面仍具韌性，但 PMI 所揭示的投入成本增速創多年新高，預示著企業利潤空間將面臨嚴峻

的輸入型通膨擠壓，這將是觀察印度經濟能否維持高成長的首要指標。
儘管短期數據出現波動，但印度作為全球增長引擎的核心地位並未動搖。在 2026-27 財政預算
案的架構下， 政府持續推動以基礎設施投資為核心的擴張性政策 ， 試圖透過提升公共支出效率來強化
製造業基礎。印度正積極利用其人口紅利與政策激勵，吸引從電子組裝到半導體測試的全球業者進
駐。然而，這種高度依賴資本流入與外部能源供給的增長模式，在地緣政治動盪與聯準會轉向鷹派的
環境下，正迎來一場壓力測試。「內部政策利多」與「外部環境利空」的互相干擾，使得印度的成長
前景短期面臨不確定性 。
能源價格震盪是印度經濟短期最重要的潛在風險。根據彭博經濟的報導，印度央行估計，原油價
格每上漲 10 美元，經濟成長將會下降 0.15%。若中東衝突導致霍爾木茲海峽關閉三個月，2027 財
年成長率恐重挫至 6.1% 。在極端情況下，燃料成本飆升將引發連鎖反應，不僅削弱消費者的實際收
入，更會因國營石油公司虧損削減股利，進而擠壓預算案中的公共建設支出與政府投資能力。除了價

格波動，供應端的物理性中斷亦是威脅。印度的能源約 85%依賴進口，若航運中斷導致供應缺口，
包含民生消費與石化產業等經濟活動都將受到供應鍊斷裂的風險。
在貨幣政策上 ， 印度央行面臨 「內外夾擊」 。 若油價衝向140 美元 ，CPI 預計將進一步升 5.0%，
並可能在 9 月突破 RBI 的 6%容忍區間，央行被迫在年底臨時升息 2 碼。外部方面，聯準會態度的轉
向鷹派加劇了資金回流美元資產的壓力，也將干擾印度央行的寬鬆節奏。綜合上述分析，我們對印度
市場看法轉向中性。雖然結構性紅利與財政擴張確保了其增長基本盤 ， 但近期的重心已由 「追求擴張」
轉向「防禦通膨與能源衝擊」，這讓 PMI 顯示內需趨緩。加上潛在的油價的大漲與供應減少，正大
幅抵銷供應鏈轉移的長期利多。我們建議在股市配置上採取守勢。在近期的估值修正期，利用市場震
盪，重新評估基本面。

拉美股市：巴西正式啟動降息循環，中東衝突有利拉美經濟
巴西央行貨幣政策委員會(Copom)於 3 月 18 日會議全票通過將基準利率調降 25 個基點至
14.75%，正式啟動新一輪降息循環。Copom 會議紀要強調該決定與當前高度不確定性的背景相符，
在地緣政治衝突持續時間與範圍難以預判 、 經濟活動放緩步調及其對物價水準影響的訊號錯綜複雜之
下，保持從容並收集額外資訊為適當之舉。Copom 也為下次會議將降息步調加速至 50 個基點保
留空間，但也強調需美伊戰爭等外部環境改善且這些事件對通膨與通膨預期影響有限才能達成。

市場通膨預期方面，截至 3 月 30 日當周巴西央行市場調查顯示，2026 年通膨預期上升 14
個基點至 4.31%，高於巴西央行 3.0% 的長期通膨目標中點，2027、2028 及 2029 年則分別為
3.84%、3.57% 及 3.50%，惟有鑑於近期油價持續走升， 預計未來數週通膨預期將可能進一步惡化。
此外，市場對 2026 年底 Selic 利率中位預期維持在 12.50%，2027 及 2028 年底分別為 10.50%
及 10.00%，顯示雖中東戰爭帶動市場通膨升溫，然而巴西市場整體降息格局並未受到太大影響，仍
是朝大幅降息的方向前進。
另一方面，雖美伊戰爭帶動短線油價上漲，帶動拉美市場通膨預期，然而油價上漲事實上也有利
於拉美整體經濟，主因拉美市場中，包含阿根廷、巴西、哥倫比亞與厄瓜多等國為石油淨出口國，高
盛研究指出若油價上漲 10%，能使整體拉美市場未來 12 個月 GDP 上升約 0.2%，顯示美伊戰爭
對拉美市場來說並非全然只有負面影響。
最後一個值得關注的議題是巴西 2026 年的總統大選，市場主要關注現任總統盧拉，與前總統

波索納洛之子弗拉維奧的對決。市調機構 Quaest 3 月民調結果顯示，在第二輪決選中，兩位候選人
首度打成平手，各獲得 41% 支持度，扭轉過往皆由盧拉勝出的局勢，顯示中東衝突引發的能源價格
上漲與通膨壓力持續影響盧拉政權的支持度 。市場預期若最終總統大選由右派陣營的弗拉維奧勝出，
其政策將會有利於巴西財政赤字問題改善，相較於盧拉政營財政擴張的策略 ，更可能吸引外資流入巴
西市場。
展望後市 ，儘管中東衝突帶動拉美市場通膨預期攀升 ，然而拉美市場最大經濟體巴西仍處於降息
循環中，且目前市場並不預期巴西央行會因為能源價格問題，而扭轉寬鬆方向，此外，拉美市場整體
亦會受惠於油價上漲帶動的石油出口助益，故整體而言，我們目前對拉美市場後市仍抱持正向看法。

能源類股：美伊停戰協商短期難有結論，可以資產配置角度布局能源類股
美伊衝突仍是 3 月主導能源價格走勢的關鍵，雖然美國在 3 月 25 日透過巴基斯坦向伊朗提出停
戰協議，然而內容與伊朗訴求相差甚遠，預料短期內美伊達成停戰協議的可能性並不高，戰事仍可能
需要來回數次的武力攻擊與談判後，才能底定。
隨戰爭持續，荷莫茲海峽的中斷程度仍是油價最關鍵的變數，戰前荷莫茲海峽每天約有 130 艘
船通過海峽 ， 目前每天僅有不到 6 艘在伊朗許可下通行，海峽運輸仍幾乎處於癱瘓狀態 。 對伊朗而言 ，
封鎖荷莫茲海峽越久 ， 越能為其換得更好的談判籌碼 ， 伊朗國會目前更是在推動立法對通過海峽的船

舶收取過路費，以此將海峽視為經濟收入來源，故何時荷莫茲海峽能夠暢通，仍具高度不確定性。
受荷莫茲海峽運量持續受阻影響，高盛 3 月 23 日報告再度上修油價預測，預估 3 月布蘭特原油
平均價格將達 105 美元，4 月將進一步上升至 115 美元，並在 2026 年第四季回落至 80 美元，該預
測基於荷莫茲海峽石油流量將在 6 週內維持極低水準的假設。此外，在海峽流量中斷 10 週的不利情
境下，高盛預估布蘭特油價將可能接近或超越 2008 年 147 美元的歷史紀錄，隨後在2026 年第四季
放緩至 100 美元；在中斷10 週並同時伴隨基礎設施受損的嚴重不利情境下，油價可能飆至接近160
美元，並在2026 年第四季仍維持 115 美元高油價。在高盛各種情境分析中，若海峽流量中斷時間延
長，或者有更多能源基礎設施受損，長期油價都存在上升的可能性，有利能源類股表現。
另一方面，觀察 Bloomberg 西德州原油期貨遠期曲線，可以看出目前市場仍定價荷莫茲海峽受
阻為中短期事件，曲線在開戰後前端快速上揚，反映現貨端供給緊張狀況，然而遠端合約變動幅度不

大，且近幾個月合約曲線陡峭，顯示市場認為荷莫茲海峽受阻仍將是短期事件，並不預期戰爭將會演
變成長期戰事，預料隨衝突結束，原油供給將大幅恢復，帶動油價下跌。3 月 30 日 WTI 遠期曲線顯
示，油價將會在 11 月回落至每桶 80 美元以下。此外，截至 3/30，國際賭盤 Polymarket 預估美伊
在 4/15 前停戰機率約 17%，4/30 前為 32%，6/30 前為 59%，12/31 前則為 75%， 機率在 4/30
至 6/30 區間增幅最大，隱含市場對停火的預期主要落在 4~6 月。
展望後市，雖然近期美伊皆提出談判條件，然而雙方在談判條件上仍存在顯著落差，再加上對於
伊朗來說，將荷莫茲海峽封鎖的越久，讓能源價格維持高檔，越能為伊朗得到更好的談判籌碼，故預
估伊朗更可能採取邊打邊談的策略，將整體談判時程拉長。對於能源類股來說，短線上若戰爭持續，
能源類股有望走出獨立行情，並可作為投資組合中對衝戰爭風險的角色，而即使在停戰後，長期油價
仍有望高於戰前水準，亦有利支撐能源類股表現，故建議可以資產配置角度，適度布局能源類股。

### Page 10

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
黃金類股：美伊戰事壓抑短線金價表現，惟反轉條件已逐步成形
黃金傳統上被視為戰爭期間的避險資產，然而本波自 2 月 28 日美伊戰爭爆發以來，金價卻明顯
表現不佳，從戰前約 5,100 美元一路下跌，一度回撤至約 4,100 美元位置。本波金價走疲，主因美伊
戰爭導致荷莫茲海峽受阻，持續推升油價，帶動市場通膨預期上升，同步推高了美國公債殖利率，使
不孳息的黃金資產吸引力下降。此外，市場避險情緒亦帶動美元走強，而黃金價格長期以來與美元走
勢呈負相關，隨美元指數走升，黃金等以美元計價之商品價格亦出現承壓。
此外，黃金重要買盤全球央行，近期購金動能也出現明顯放緩。世界黃金協會報告指出，2026

年 1 月全球央行淨購金量驟降至 5 噸，較去年月均購金量 27 噸銳減近 80%，推測主因 1 月金價波
動過大，導致各國央行態度轉為觀望。 另一方面，土耳其央行近期大幅減持黃金 ，也對市場情緒造
成壓抑。據彭博新聞引述知情人士報導，土耳其央行在 3 月 13 日至 3 月 20 日的兩周內，以出售及
協議交換置換了約 60 噸黃金，用以穩定因美伊戰爭而受影響的土耳其里拉匯率，彭博指出，土耳其
央行本次減持黃金，一部分是採市場直接拋售，一部分則是通過 SWAP 以黃金作為抵押品來獲取外
匯或里拉 ，而歷史上各國央行透過 SWAP 以貴金屬獲得廉價美元融資的情況並不少見，且回顧 2023
年 3 月至 5 月期間，土耳其央行亦曾售出近 160 噸黃金，惟當時因來自黃金 ETF 的買家吸收賣壓，
對短線金價並未造成太大影響，故若僅有來自土耳其央行的一次性賣壓，實際並不需要太過擔憂。
更需要留意的是 ， 中東戰爭將可能迫使其他國家同樣採取出售黃金儲備的動作，以支撐外匯存底
或籌措資金，若此趨勢擴大，將 可能進一步增加黃金短線賣壓， 同時，因目前市場資金較不偏好黃

金，短線上黃金 ETF 吸納來自各國央行賣壓的力道也會較弱，故只要美伊戰爭持續，黃金類股短線
表現將可能受到較大壓力。
即便如此，我們仍可看出世界央行對中長期增持黃金儲備抱持正向態度 ，例如在 1 月份的世界央
行黃金買家中，就意外的出現了馬來西亞央行，為該國自 2018 年以來首度增持黃金儲備，另外在 3
月初，韓國央行也宣布計畫從 2026 年第一季起，將黃金 ETF 納入其外匯準備投資組合，顯示各國
央行對於增持黃金儲備的長期方向仍未改變 。世界黃金協會也預估 ，預期全球央行在 2026 年將會增
持約 850 噸黃金，增持水準將會與去年相近，顯示未來世界央行購金買盤仍可望為金價帶來支撐。
此外，展望後市 ，金價反轉的條件實際上也已在逐步成形，當前市場直覺認為高油價將導致通膨 ，
預期聯準會動作會偏鷹，導致金價表現受到壓抑。然而若戰事在短期內結束，預料市場將忽略短期
油價上漲的影響，使降息預期大致回升到戰前水準，有利金價表現；而若美伊戰爭 進一步擴大，將
可能使油價被推升至壓抑經濟表現的價格，屆時經濟更可能面臨的問題反而不是通膨而是衰退， 聯

準會更可能降息以救經濟，隨利率走低亦有利金價反彈。故 目前來看，隨金價回檔已深，重回多頭
條件也已在逐步成形，建議短線可持續觀望戰事變化，中性看待黃金類股後市表現。

### Page 11

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
固定收益：通膨疑慮雖推升利率，未來仍有降息可能
FOMC 3/18 會議釋出訊號略偏鷹派。投票結果 11 比 1，僅 Miran 主張降息一碼，而原先偏鴿
的 Waller 轉為支持按兵不動，內部對降息的共識正在弱化。會後聲明雖新增中東局勢不確定性的描
述，釋出政策需保持耐心的訊號，但整體對經濟評估仍維持「穩健」 ，未因2 月就業數據轉弱而顯著
調整措辭。點陣圖中位數雖未變動，但分布結構出現轉變。首先，預期降息的官員人數減少，支持多
次降息的比例下滑；其次，已出現個別官員預期未來升息的情境；此外，長期利率中位數上修，顯示

中性利率評估提高。
雖然因中東能源問題導致 Fed Funds 隔夜利率交換市場所估算今年底前的利率變動預期已從戰
事爆發前的降息 2 碼大幅縮減，近期更出現不排除升息的臆測。然而，自美以伊衝突以來至 3/26(台
北時間)， 包括高盛在內的投資銀行所發布對Fed 利率(區間上緣)預期均認為 FOMC 未來兩年仍將傾
向降息。若此預期得以實現，代表市場目前已過度反映 Fed 的「鷹派情境」，之後殖利率可望有回
穩的空間。
再者，3 月油價上揚，投資人對於未來美國貨幣政策轉向的疑慮升溫，確實也導致了美國甚至是
全球的主要債券市場殖利率上揚。然而，就在市場聚焦於通膨的同時，包括 Pimco、摩根大通和
Columbia Threadneedle Investments 等投資機構 3/30 不約而同表達，投資人低估了美國對伊朗
戰爭導致經濟急劇放緩的風險的看法，並認為這場衝擊最終將引發債券殖利率再度回落 。高盛 3 月下
旬的報告提醒，比起升息，中東戰事更大的風險在於(接近)經濟衰退，這可能觸發 Fed 更大幅度的降

息。Pimco 首席投資官 Daniel Ivascyn 也表示「通常始於通膨衝擊的局面，會迅速演變為成長衝擊…
而我們正處於經濟大幅走軟的邊緣。」因此，未來債券殖利率不排除有向下的可能性存在。
隨著美國 11 月期中選舉逼近，川普在政治上將面臨更大的壓力，以美國財政體質及川普向來的
政策來看，之後美國政府勢必將盡力壓低美國公債的殖利率，有利使美債利率處於相對可控的範圍。
近期美國公債殖利率雖因市場的通膨疑慮而上揚 ， 但目前殖利率遠高於Fed 長期通膨目標(2%)，
美債的高息收對長線投資人具強大誘因 ， 因此投資人仍可以資產配置角度對於債券作適當之配置 。 此
外，包括 Pimco、摩根大通等投資機構也認為未來債券殖利率有向下的可能性存在。以目前的殖利
率看，美債仍有投資空間。考量短期殖利率受市場對 Fed 的利率政策預期變動影響較大，故在年期
與券種的選擇上可以 7~15 年的中長期美國公債或投資級債為主。

### Page 3

Cathay’s View市場策略思維
市場焦點
1. 市場焦點：
• 美伊情勢與超級央行週：美伊衝突持續, 荷姆茲海峽維持封鎖, 紅海也遭遇葉門胡塞組織襲擊, 油價上
沖下洗, 美、英、日央行會議, 面臨油價與通膨擔憂, 央行維持謹慎態度。
• 科技財報分析, 5大CSP正面臨現金荒：科技巨頭財報表現不俗, 雲端成長加速, 大廠持續加大投資, 已
面臨自由現金流翻負的情境。
• AI缺貨牌失靈? 台日韓股面臨殺融資：5大CSP自由現金流轉負, 市場擔憂高成長難持續, 籌碼鬆動下,
股價仍需整理。
2. 後續觀察重點：
• ISM製造業、非農就業人數、台灣出口。
策略思維
1. 股市-拉回仍站買方思考：股市獲利了結賣壓及評價偏高恐使得震盪加劇, 但企業獲利成長加速, AI需求強勁
下仍具強勁支撐, 宜採取分批方式布局。
2. 債市-Fed觀望戰事影響：Fed觀望中東戰事及油價上漲影響, 升息預期走揚, 10年債殖利率高檔震盪, 採取逢
高鎖利策略進行配置。
3. 匯市-美元高檔震盪：美元指數近期受中東不確定性而維持高檔震盪格局。

川普政策軟化機率較高
川普TACO指數 近期油價走勢隨消息面上沖下洗
原油｜中東動盪延續, 但原油走升的強度卻已不像先前, 而股市對於中東地緣態勢, 也逐漸鈍化
• 中東最新局勢: 在經歷連續近兩週的密集轟炸與報復攻擊後, 美伊雙方自週末起已暫時停止互射飛彈與空襲。儘管
波斯灣正面衝突放緩, 但葉門胡塞武裝上週對紅海油輪發動新一輪攻擊, 導致曼德海峽航運再次放緩, 也讓市場持
續觀望事態發展。
• 觀察近期川普TACO指數(Trump Always Chickens Out Index)明顯回升, 反映金融市場與油價波動所累積的政策壓
力已達相對高位。依歷史經驗, 該指標飆升意味著白宮因顧及通膨與選情, 極可能在近期放緩極限施壓並轉向談判。
展望後市, 若美伊談判釋出實質降級訊號, 地緣溢價可望加速消退；惟目前荷姆茲海峽疑慮尚未完全消除, 油價短
線仍將維持上沖下洗的態勢。
註: TACO指數用來量化川普決策的綜合指標, 追蹤變數包含股市、殖利率、油價等。

政策利率連五凍, 美債利率維持高檔 記者會重點對比

聯準會｜7月FOMC維持利率不變, 鷹派分歧顯現
• 7月會議以9:3的票數維持利率不變 。Hammack、Kashkari與Logan支持本月應升息, Warsh重申為良好的家庭紛
爭(Family Fight), 委員會對「穩定價格」的目標一致, 但對最佳策略看法不同。
• 聯準會進入「警覺思考」(Watchful thinking)階段, 希望市場盯著球打(數據), 而非裁判(聯準會)。在Fed減少前瞻
指引後, 市場已自行推升公債殖利率, 聯準會希望能觀察金融市場已傳遞的緊縮效果, 對實體經濟的傳導影響。
• 展望未來, 關鍵在於荷姆茲海峽實質解封進度。若解封延宕, 推升第三季平均油價於80美元上方, 相較去年同期約
65美元(年增率逾2成), 恐加劇9月升息風險; 反之, 若運輸恢復順利, 則有助緩解聯準會升息緊縮壓力。

• 由於市場已提前反映今年升息預期 , FOMC會後市場對年底利率預估略有下修 , 但9月升息機率仍達 63%、12月

險逐步降溫, 且聯準會立場轉趨溫和, 屆時殖利率方有自高檔回落的空間。
• 各天期美債利率仍處高位, 2年: 4.26%、10年: 4.68%, 30年更一舉突破5.2%。市場升息預期已大幅反映, 使近期

利率曲線上行主要由長天期領漲; 在美國債務壓力增大、未來聯準會縮表影響下, 預期殖利率曲線維持陡峭化格局。

4大CSP雲端部門營收 CSP大規模資本支出熱潮尚未放緩
註: 4大CSP包含微軟、谷歌、亞馬遜、Meta。
資料來源: Bloomberg預估。
註: 5大CSP包含微軟、谷歌、亞馬遜、Meta、甲骨文。
資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 高盛與Morgan Stanley預估。
• 微軟財報亮眼, 雲端部門Intelligent Cloud營收年增31.6%較前季29.6%再加速, 此外, 微軟預計從2027財年起(即
2026Q3起), 將資料中心預計使用年限從15年延長到25年, 並調整會計認列方式, 資料中心建置成本不再列入資本
支出, 因此微軟預估2026年資本支出將從1900億美元降至1750億美元。雲端營收成長優於預期、自由現金流高於

預期, 激勵微軟盤後股價大漲近9%。
• Meta營收年增28%略優於預期, 但一次性的訴訟費用侵蝕EPS, 上季GAAP EPS 6.18美元, 低於預期的7.15美元。
此外, Q3預估營收625億美元, 低於預期, 並小幅提高全年預估資本支出下限至1300億美元, 盤後股價重挫7.5%。
• 微軟強調, 雲端服務產能仍供不應求, 將持續擴大AI投資, 微軟剩餘履約義務突破6780億美元, 亞馬遜4960億美元,
谷歌5140億美元, AI基礎建設大舉擴充仍是趨勢。

註: 5大CDS以平均權重計算。
資料來源: Bloomberg。 資料來源: Reuters預估。
5大CSP CDS vs IG殖利率觀察 5大CSP淨利與自由現金流預測
• 隨著資料中心建設持續擴大, 5大CSP的自由現金流自2026年起逐季下滑, 並於2027年落入明顯負值區間。這反映
的並非本業獲利惡化, 而是企業選擇將龐大的營運現金流再次投入AI擴張。短期財務指標承壓, 信用市場也如實反
映這股轉變。5大CSP CDS指數自年初約60點一路攀升至接近100點, 惡化幅度明顯高於整體企業CDS指數。
• 隨著5大CSP CDS指數飆高, 加上近期公債殖利率上行, 整體投資等級債的殖利率也呈現上行, 由於5大CSP仍具備

強勁的現金創造能力、領先的市場地位及充裕的融資彈性, 即使階段性惡化, 整體信用體質依然穩健, 且整體投等
債CDS並未隨之走升, 顯示信用風險仍低, 殖利率上移反而是債市逢高鎖利的機會。

正, 股市由過熱回到中性位階。

• 美股財報週, 企業獲利表現優異, 標普500企業營益率創5年來新高, 或可歸功於AI降本增益的效益, 然而4大CSP業
者雲端營收大增, 營業利益持平, 但自由現金流轉負, 也引發大舉投資AI, 可能擴大債務、增加利息支出以及提高折
舊的疑慮。不過若以在手訂單而言, CSP業者仍需加緊投資以滿足大量的算力需求, 因此獲利趨勢仍強勁上行。
• 科技巨頭獲利無虞, 但股價卻因擴大舉債擔憂而下跌, 若以今年以來股價表現7巨頭仍處於下跌區間, 落後標普其他
493檔, 顯示評價面反而下降, 在股市修正之際可以逢低布局。

HBM的領先者, 在股價劇烈修正後具備長期投資價值。

韓股｜槓桿去化過程長於預期, 韓股跌勢未止
• 韓國槓桿去化過程仍未告終 , 即便融資餘額持續降低但跌勢仍然延續 。此外, 外資今年對於韓股均持續站在賣方 ,

議將資金分為多筆分批布局。

長鑫科技IPO, 持續推動自主化進程 中港股走勢
中國｜長鑫科技正式掛牌, 市場反應熱烈, 但國際氣氛影響下, 拖累A股走勢
• 長鑫科技以8.66元/股IPO, 受惠記憶體需求大漲, 2026Q1營收達508億元(年增719%), 淨利潤轉虧為盈達330億元,
讓7/27在上海科創板掛牌當日, 股價大漲至49元/股。而本次募資申購倍數達212倍, 反應熱烈。該上市不僅是單純
IPO, 更是中國供應鏈里程碑: 補齊「自主可控」的拼圖、AI硬體本土化的承載者。
• 美媒報導中國今年將交付5台自製浸沒式DUV, 2027年擴至20台, 顧客涵蓋中芯與長鑫。雖然ASML每年僅浸沒式
DUV出貨就達130台以上, 中國國產機台初期僅雙位數交付量, 對 ASML全球營收衝擊僅約2%, 實質影響有限, 但此
舉象徵中國設備廠「0 到 1」的突破、ASML獨佔地位微微動搖, 也驗證中國「國家隊＋資本」模式的推行。
• 市場面: 長鑫IPO雖激勵A股, 但近期國際市場風險氣氛不佳, 半導體股頻遭賣壓, 使A股也遭拖累。長線, 中國晶片

自給仍遠遠不足, 意味著硬體訂單無虞, 供應鏈自主為主軸。因此, AI趨勢不變下, AI概念股拉回仍視為布局機會。

公司債利差小幅走擴, 仍處歷史相對低位 US IG: 各產業利差與YTD變化
資料來源: Bloomberg。 資料來源: Bloomberg。
信用債｜市場重新定價Hyperscalers槓桿風險, 利差擴張提升配置價值
• 近期信用債表現承壓, 除市場上修升息預期外, 今年Hyperscalers(大型雲端服務商)大舉發債, 以及Alphabet自由現
金流轉負值, 引發市場對AI資本支出與槓桿擴張的關注。美國IG利差擴張至80bps, 惟年初至今整體表現大致持平。
• 本輪利差擴張反映債務供給增加, 而非基本面惡化。考量Hyperscalers獲利前景仍具韌性, 預期償債能力穩健、違
約風險有限, 利差走擴反提供鎖利良機。產業配置偏好金融、科技與通訊產業; 金融受惠M&A回溫、手續費及交易
收入改善, 以及監管環境趨於鬆綁; 科技與通訊則提供較佳收益補償, 惟需留意短線交易層面上的利差擴張風險。

英國官方利率、公債、與油價 歐系貨幣兌美元走勢
資料來源: Bloomberg。 資料來源: Bloomberg。
• 英國央行認為英國內生通膨其實正在降溫, 但中東能源衝擊使未來幾季通膨仍有反彈風險, 因此選擇維持3.75% 利
率並保留偏鷹派立場(7:2、本次6:3中, 首席經濟學家Pill加入導致市場重視內部研究對通膨風險的評估)。但整體聲
明提到能源衝擊至今尚未引發廣泛的「第二輪通膨效應」或間接影響, 且2027年的薪資協商看起來也相對溫和, 多
數企業計畫將2027年的薪資調幅放緩至2~4%的區間。
• 短線上利率的浮動多半受到油價的同步牽引居多, 各國皆同。在利率不變的基礎上, 英鎊仍將受到政治的動向影響
較大, 從總理變動政治負面風險到新總理財政正面溢酬, 後續將是常態回歸的走法, 英鎊重新貼合歐元機率較大, 而
對美元的的影響反而得到被動推升動能。

日圓、韓元同一時間出現急貶, 干預可能性極高 近期主要亞幣升貶值方向迥異

• 儘管聯準會並未升息, 但3票的反對票與油價高檔震盪, 仍讓市場持續擔憂聯準會年內升息可能, 帶動美元指數高檔

## Document 186: [000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 변하지 않은 것SK하이닉스(000660) 변하지 않은 것변하지 않은 것 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 변하지 않은 것SK하이닉스(000660) 변하지 않은 것변하지 않은 것 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-07-30
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

00

SK 하이닉스 (000660/KS) 
변하지 않은 것 
2Q26 영업이익 61 조원 (+19% QoQ) 기록 
SK하이닉스의 2Q26 영업이익은 61조원 (+19% QoQ)으로 SK증권 예상치 (60.7
조원)에 부합했으나, 시장컨센서스 대비 소폭 하회했다. 주요 원인은 고부가 메모리의 
본격 출하가 3Q26으로 이연된 영향이다. 출하는 DRAM +9%, NAND +14%, 판가
는 DRAM +29%, NAND +54%, 영업이익은 DRAM 46조원, NAND 15조원으로 
추정된다. 영업이익률은 DRAM 79%, NAND 69%로 HBM4 본격화 이전, 경쟁사대
비 높은 HBM 비중에 따른 불리한 믹스에도 불구하고, 여전히 업계 최고 수익성이다.

업황 강세 지속 
SK 증권은 시장 우려에도 불구하고, 증익 기조는 전혀 변하지 않았다고 판단한다. 현재 
업계의 재고 수준은 공급자 3 주, 하이퍼스케일러 3주, 채널 5-7주 수준으로 정상 범
주 대비 절반 수준에 불과하며, 수요 충족률은 70% 미만으로 파악된다. LTA효과는 
성장 종료가 아닌, 수요 예측력 제고와 실적 지속성 제고, 이중시장 효과에 따른 비 
LTA 및 현물 시장의 가격 탄력성 제고이다. 제한적 공간 여력, HBM 및 TLC 강세에 
따른 웨이퍼 비트 효율 감소 등을 AI 사이클에서 구조적 현상이며, 공급 부족은 2027
년에 더욱 심화될 것이다. 메모리 가격 상승과 전통 세트 위축에 따른 가격 저항이 목격
되고 있으나, 공급자들은 AI 서버 향 생산 능력 배분 확대로 대응하고 있다. 공급자들
의 세트향 비중이 30% 이하로 하락하는 과정에 있다는 점, AI 투자 기조 지속을 감안
하면, 세트에서 나타나는 신호를 전체화 시키는 것은 성급한 오류이다. SK 하이닉스의 
3Q26 DRAM 가격 역시 시장 우려와 달리 +19% QoQ로 예상을 상회할 것이다.

목표주가, 실적 전망 모두 유지 
SK증권은 SK하이닉스에 대한 목표주가 400만원을 유지한다. 시장 금리 상승에 대
한 우려와 CDS 프리미엄 상승 등 AI 밸류에 불리한 환경에도 불구하고 목표주가를 유
지하는 이유는 다음과 같다. ① 26, 27년 실적 전망 유지 (각각 274조원, 425조원), 
② AI 설비투자에 대한 지속성 (낮은 급락 가능성; 과거 수요와 다른 성격, 명분), ③ 
LTA를 통한 이익 가시성, 지속성, 창출력 제고, ④ 하반기 주주환원 시작이다. 과거와 
다른 업황을 인정받기 위해서는 주주환원이 가장 중요하며, ‘실적 성장률’이 아닌 ‘이익 
창출력, 지속력’을 기반으로 평가받는 중요한 변곡점이 될 것이다. 26, 27, 28년 SK하
이닉스의 FCF는 150조원, 261조원, 346조원 수준으로 전망되며, 주주환원의 가시화
는 순현금 100조원 달성이 예상되는 3Q26 실적 발표 또는 그 이전을 기대한다.

본 보고서에 언급된 종목의 경우 당사 조사분석담당자는 본인의 담당종목을 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
본 보고서는 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공된 사실이 없습니다. 
당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다. 
종목별 투자의견은 다음과 같습니다.

주: K-IFRS연결요약재무제표
[투자포인트]
■ 현대차 26.2Q 매출액 48.0억원 (-0.5% YoY), 영업이익 2.8조원 (-20.1% YoY, 6.0% OPM) 전망. 현대차
26.2Q 국내 도매 판매의 경우 158,515대 (-15.9% YoY)를 기록. 안전 공업 화재 관련 생산 차질 영향이
존재하고, 그룹사 대비 생산 차질 영향이 주력 차종에 집중된 것으로 파악. 26.06 현대차 국내 출고 대수는
60,367대를 기록했고, 26.3Q부터 생산 차질 영향은 미미할 것. 기말 환율이 상승함에 따른 판매보증충당금

추가 인식분이 2,000억원 내외 발생할 전망이고, 원자재 가격 상승 영향도 여전히 존재하는 것으로 파악됨.
■ 26.2Q 미국 도매 판매 265,208대 (+2.0% YoY)를 기록. 전년 동기 대비 미국 판매 성장폭이 감소하고 있는
것은 사실이고, 인센티브 상승 압박도 상당한 것으로 파악됨. 단 26.03 일부 트림에서의 리콜 사태가 발생한
펠리세이드 판매 업황이 준수하다는 점이 인상적. 26.2Q 펠리세이드 미국 판매 대수는 35,938대 (+26.5%
YoY)를 기록. 이는 26.2H 출시 예정된 볼룸 모델 신차 효과와 함께 올해의 성장 동인이 될 것으로 전망.
■ 현대차 기업가치는 1) 본업, 2) SDV/자율주행 경쟁력, 3) Boston Dynamics 등 세가지 요소로 구성되고, 이 중
Boston Dynamics의 비중이 높다는 점은 분명. 연초부터 주가 급등 국면이 관찰되었던 이유는 시장이 Boston

Dynamics 경쟁력과 향후 사업 로드맵에 공감하였기 때문. 시장 변동성이 확대되며 주가 조정 국면이 이어지고
있으나, 28년 양산 공장 완공 및 RMAC 가동 등의 주요 로드맵은 견조함에 따라 목표주가 650,000원을 유지.

Compliance notice
당보고서공표일기준으로해당기업과관련하여,
회사는해당종목을1%이상보유하고있지않습니다.
금융투자분석사와그배우자는해당기업의주식을보유하고있지않습니다.
당보고서는기관투자가및제3자에게E-mail등을통하여사전에배포된사실이없습니다.

회사는6개월간해당기업의유가증권발행과관련주관사로참여하지않았습니다.
당보고서에게재된내용들은본인의의견을정확하게반영하고있으며,외부의부당한압력이나간섭없이
작성되었음을확인합니다.
본분석자료는투자자의증권투자를돕기위한참고자료이며,따라서,본자료에의한투자자의투자결과에대해
어떠한목적의증빙자료로도사용될수없으며,어떠한경우에도작성자및당사의허가없이전재,복사또는
대여될수없습니다.무단전재등으로인한분쟁발생시법적책임이있음을주지하시기바랍니다.
[투자의견]
종목추천투자등급
종목투자의견은향후12개월간추천일종가대비해당종목의예상목표수익률을의미함.
Buy(매수):추천일종가대비+15%이상
Hold(보유):추천일종가대비-15% ~ 15%내외등락
 Sell(매도):추천일종가대비-15%이상

2Q26 Review: 영업이익 컨센서스 상회 
동사 2026년 2분기 연결 실적은 매출액 4조 7,248억원(+3.4% YoY), 영업이익 3,143억원
(+15.9% YoY, OPM 6.7%) 으로 영업이익 컨센서스 2,629억원을 20% 상회하였다. 수익 
개선은 에너빌리티부문의 매출 증가('25~'26년에 수주한 대형 가스발전 프로젝트 및 원자력 
기자재의 공정 초과 영향)와 밥캣의 관세 효과에 기인하였다. 
에너빌리티부문은 매출액 2조 2,330억원(-1.5% YoY), 영업이익 974억원(+5.4% YoY, 
OPM 4.4)을 기록하였다. 상반기 수주는 견조한 가스터빈 계약(가스터빈 12기, 스팀터빈 6
기, 서비스 5건)과 오만 두큼 가스복합발전소 건설공사(약 5,300억원, 6 월 수주)로 연간 수
주 목표(13.3조)를 54% 달성하였다. 가스/수소는 이미 97%(목표 3.2조), 복합EPC는 
63%(목표 3조)를 달성한 만큼, 가이던스 초과 달성 가능성도 있다고 판단한다. 
동트기 전이 가장 어둡다 
하반기에는 팀코리아의 체코 두코바니 원전 시공 계약 및 SMR 기자재 계약 체결이 전망된
다. SMR은 연내 X-energy에 장주기 품목 공급 가능성이 있다고 판단한다. 또한 미국 에너
지부에서 최대 5개의 AP1000 프로젝트에 대한 175억 달러 규모의 금융 지원을 발표한 만
큼, AP1000 발주 가시성이 높아졌다고 판단한다. 당사 추정치에는 보수적으로 반영
(AP1000 1기 반영)해 두었으나, 해당 프로젝트의 특수목적법인(SPV) 설립 가속화에 따라 
하반기 추정치 상향도 기대해 볼 수 있다. 
투자의견 Buy 유지, 목표주가 149,000원 유지 
동사에 대해 투자의견 Buy를 유지하고, 목표주가는 149,000원을 유지한다. 8~9월 중 발표
될 것으로 전망되는 대미투자 1호의 투자대상은 에너지(SMR, LNG 등)로 결정될 가능성이 
높다고 판단한다. 아울러 연말에 발표 예정인 12차 전기본(전력수급기본계획)에는 3대 메가 
프로젝트로 인한 추가 전력 수요(기존 잠정수요 전망안 대비 약 30GW 이상 확대 전망)가 
반영될 것으로 전망한다. 당사는 아직 대미투자 및 12차 전기본의 구체적인 계획과 발전원
별 규모 및 mix가 확정되지 않은 만큼 이를 추정치에 반영하지는 않았으나, 동사가 원전과 
가스터빈 사업을 모두 영위하는 만큼, 추후 기대할 수 있는 업사이드가 충분하다고 판단한
다.

## Document 209: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 항체를 넘어 펩타이드로삼성바이오로직스(207940) 항체를 넘어 펩타이드로항체를 넘어 펩타이드로 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 항체를 넘어 펩타이드로삼성바이오로직스(207940) 항체를 넘어 펩타이드로항체를 넘어 펩타이드로 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

기업분석 2026.07.21 www.daishin.com

Issue Comment 
삼성바이오

(207940) 
 항체를 넘어 펩타이드로 
홍가혜

kahye0.hong@daishin.com

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 목표주가 2,000,000 
유지  
현재주가 
(26.07.20) 
1,345,000 
제약업종

◼ 2.7조원 규모의 폴리펩타이드 인수로 펩타이드 CDMO 진출 
◼ 차세대 모달리티 경쟁력 강화 및 글로벌 생산거점 확보 
◼ 중장기 전략에는 긍정적, 거래 종결 및 통합 과정 확인 필요

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
삼성바이오로직스는 7/20 공시를 통해 약 2.7조원 규모의 스위스 펩타이드 
CDMO 업체 폴리펩타이드 그룹(SIX: PPGN) 인수를 발표함. 이번 인수를 통
해 기존 항체 중심 CDMO에서 펩타이드로 모달리티를 확대하고, 미국▪유럽▪
인도 생산거점을 확보했다는 점에서 중장기 성장 전략에 부합하는 긍정적인 
이벤트로 판단함. 특히 GLP-1 기반 비만치료제 시장 성장으로 수요가 확대
되는 펩타이드 시장에 M&A를 통해 즉시 진입해 자체 증설 대비 시장 진입 
기간을 단축했다는 점도 긍정적임. 다만, 거래 종결 이후 통합 시너지의 구체
화가 필요한 것으로 판단되어 목표주가에는 미반영함.

폴리펩타이드 그룹은 글로벌 Top-tier 펩타이드 CDMO 
폴리펩타이드 그룹은 약 70년 업력의 펩타이드 전문 CDMO로, 미국▪유럽▪인
도 등 5개국에서 6개의 cGMP 생산거점을 운영 중임. 1,000개 이상의 펩타이
드 의약품 생산 경험과 글로벌 임상3상 펩타이드 파이프라인의 약 1/3을 확
보하고 있어, 향후 상업화 전환에 따른 생산 물량 증가가 기대됨. ‘25년 매출
은 3.89억 유로를 기록했으며, 대사질환 매출 비중이 빠르게 확대 중임(‘21년 
22%→’25년 57%). ‘26년 매출 성장률 20-25%, EBITDA 마진 mid-to-high 
teens를 가이던스로 제시함.

전략적 의미: 모달리티+생산거점 확대를 통한 성장동력 다변화 
이번 인수의 핵심은 펩타이드 모달리티와 글로벌 생산거점을 동시에 확보했
다는 것임. GLP-1 중심의 고성장 시장에 즉시 진입하는 동시에 기존 생산시
설과 고객 기반을 승계해 인수 직후 매출 기여가 가능하며, 미국▪유럽▪인도 
거점을 기반으로 공급망 경쟁력도 강화될 전망임. 또한 양사가 보유한 글로벌 
고객 기반을 활용한 교차 수주와 운영 효율화를 통한 중장기 수익성 개선도 
기대됨.

중장기 전략에는 긍정적, 거래 종결 및 통합 과정 확인 필요 
이번 인수는 연내 마무리를 목표로 하며, 공개매수는 9월 개시 이후 약 2개
월간 진행될 예정임. 최대주주의 공개매수 응모 확약으로 거래 성사 가능성은 
높은 것으로 판단함. 인수자금은 보유 현금과 영업현금흐름으로 상당 부분 충
당 가능할 전망임. 다만, 6공장 및 송도 제3캠퍼스 투자 등 병행되는 대규모 
CAPEX를 감안하면 추가 차입 가능성은 존재하며, 향후 거래 종결과 자금조
달 방식은 확인이 필요함.

KOSPI 6,516.27

## Document 227: AI盈利兑现主导交易，亚马逊涨15%力撑纳指收涨，苹果重挫7%，30年美债收益率涨破5.27%

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI盈利兑现主导交易，亚马逊涨15%力撑纳指收涨，苹果重挫7%，30年美债收益率涨破5.27%.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-07-31
Pages: 13
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

标普涨 0.70% ，纳指涨 1% 。中概股指收涨 1.47% 。亚⻢逊创 2012 年以来最⼤单⽇涨幅。苹
果创 2025 年 4 ⽉份以来最⼤单⽇跌幅。美光跌 5.90% 。 10 年期美债收益率升约 6 个基点⾄
4.74% ，⽉内飙升逾 30 个基点，创 2005 年以来最⼤七⽉涨幅。美元兑⽇元跌 1% 、跌破 158
关⼝。现货⻩⾦跌 1.27% 。 WTI 原油涨近 1.3% 。
摘要：
亚⻢逊财报强化 AI 投⼊开始兑现的预期，抵消苹果弱指引及美债利率上⾏的压⼒，美股三⼤指数
震荡收⾼。
亚⻢逊发布季度业绩后股价单⽇暴涨逾 15% ，成为当⽇市场最⼤提振⼒量，带动标普 500 指数上
涨 0.7% ，纳斯达克上涨 1% ， " 科技七巨头 " 整体涨幅达 3.2% 。
微软跟涨 3% ，延续周四逾 15% 暴涨后的强势，两家超⼤规模云计算巨头的靓丽财报在⼀定程度
上平息了市场对 AI 基础设施投资能否获得回报的疑虑。
然⽽，单⽇的反弹难以掩盖整个 7 ⽉的市场溃败。标普 500 七⽉基本持平，纳斯达克⽉跌 3.2% ，

后者创下 2004 年以来最差七⽉表现。
10 年期美债收益率⽉内飙升逾 30 个基点，创 2005 年以来最⼤七⽉涨幅； WTI 原油⽉涨逾 20% ，
创 30 年来最⼤七⽉涨幅。中东冲突再度升级触发的能源冲击，是贯穿整个⽉份的核⼼叙事。
亚⻢逊领衔科技反弹，苹果重挫拖累指数
周五美股三⼤指数震荡收⾼。标普 500 涨 0.70% ，纳指涨 1% ，道指涨 0.53% 。中概股指跑赢⼤
盘、收涨 1.47% ，本周累计涨 8.09% ， 7 ⽉累计上涨 12.84% 。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

亚⻢逊周五业绩报告显⽰其季度营收录得逾四年来最⼤增速，股价随即飙升逾 15% 。
CEO Andy Jassy 对 AI 数据中⼼投资的清晰阐述，打消了市场此前对于 " ⽆序烧钱 " 的担忧，有效
接续了微软周四传递的正向信号。此前微软展⽰了 Azure 加速增⻓、云端利润率稳定以及 Copilot
变现的初步证据。
Longbow Asset Management CEO Jake Dollarhide 表⽰：
与此同时，苹果因供应约束警告及 iPhone 提价或抑制需求的悲观展望，股价单⽇下跌 7.4% 。
市场担⼼亚⻢逊的⽀出只是天⻢⾏空的赌博，⽽ Andy Jassy ⽤业绩打消了这些疑虑。

### Page 3

AI交易并未带来全板块普涨。周五芯⽚股整体表现平淡，费城半导体指数微涨 0.07% ，但仍较 6
⽉ 22 ⽇历史⾼点下跌逾 20% 。
美股存储芯⽚指数⽇欸跌 1.58% 。美光盘初⾼开、⼀度上涨 6.40% ，随后转为下跌，收盘跌
5.90% 。 SK 海⼒⼠ ADR 跌 3.54% 。
标普 500 指数经历了⾃ 2014 年以来最糟糕的 7 ⽉表现，纳斯达克指数经历了⾃ 2004 年以来最糟糕
的 7 ⽉表现。

### Page 4

等权重标普 500 指数，以及剔除⼈⼯智能成分股后的指数创下历史新⾼。
能源板块为 7 ⽉股市最⼤赢家，科技与材料板块领跌，利率敏感的公⽤事业和房地产板块同样承
压。
⼋⽉进⼊视野，历史季节性规律本就不利于股市，加之中东局势悬⽽未决、美联储政策路径分
歧加深以及⾼杠杆资⾦的去化是否已经完成，这些都将是投资者需要持续追踪的关键变量。
7 ⽉动量崩塌、杠杆仓位出清

### Page 5

尽管主要股指跌幅有限，但指数内部经历了剧烈震荡。
动量因⼦ 7 ⽉暴跌 25% ，创 2009 年 5 ⽉以来最差单⽉表现，其中动量强势股组合⽉内下跌 21% 。
据⾼盛 Prime 经纪数据，过去三个交易⽇出现⾃ 2022 年 11 ⽉以来最⼤规模的单边去杠杆，基⾦正
在⼤规模出清 4 ⽉⾄ 5 ⽉间建⽴的杠杆头⼨，尤其集中于内存芯⽚及半导体持仓。
⾼盛的 AI 受益股与承压股对冲组合在⽉内⼤幅回撤，光学⽹络与数据中⼼板块跌幅居前。
零售热⻔股 7 ⽉同样遭受重创。

### Page 6

不过，得益于微软和亚⻢逊的亮眼财报，超⼤规模云计算板块本周全⾯爆发，跑赢纳斯达克 100
指数的幅度创下历史新⾼。从⽉度表现来看，微软和亚⻢逊分别斩获了超 20% 和 13% 的显著涨
幅。
Bespoke 投资集团策略师提醒，投资者当前⾯临的核⼼问题是：
⽬前来看，⾼盛估计 AI Alpha 策略在 2026 年上半年贡献了多头 / 空头股票对冲基⾦ 85% 的总
Alpha ，但这⼀主导地位正在逆转，内存股拥挤程度仍接近历史纪录⾼位，若超⼤规模云⼚商资
本⽀出指引出现任何令市场失望的信号，将带来进⼀步下⾏⻛险。
七⽉核⼼叙事：能源冲击引爆连锁反应
中东原油供应何时恢复正常仍有不确定性。周五布伦特原油收于 87.93 美元，上涨 1.20% 。
即期布伦特原油现货价格在 7 ⽉份也⼤幅飙升，重回 91 美元以上，即期期货曲线呈现看涨的逆价
差。
AI 相关板块的疲软究竟是⻓期拐点，还是仅仅是组合再平衡？

### Page 7

此前 6 ⽉达成的停⽕协议在 7 ⽉初彻底破裂，伊朗军队袭击霍尔⽊兹海峡内的商业船只，特朗普
随即于 7 ⽉ 7 ⽇⾄ 8 ⽇前后宣布停⽕终⽌，美军对伊朗沿海防御、导弹阵地及海军资产展开新⼀轮
空袭。
⽉中⾄⽉末，霍尔⽊兹海峡这⼀全球能源贸易最关键的咽喉要道仍然遭受严重⼲扰，⼀度出现
间歇性封锁。
与此同时，乌克兰对俄罗斯炼油⼚的远程⽆⼈机打击摧毁了俄约 30% ⾄ 45% 的在役炼化产能，
欧洲柴油精炼利润率随之升破 60 美元 / 桶，全球精炼产品价格逼近战时⾼位。
债券遭重创，美联储政策路径悬⽽未决
能源冲击推升通胀预期，进⽽重创美国国债市场。
10 年期美债收益率⽉内上⾏逾 30 个基点，本周⼀度触及 5.27% ，为 2007 年以来最⾼⽔平；周五
报 4.71% ，当⽇上⾏ 3 个基点。

### Page 8

德国 10 年期国债收益率当⽇上⾏ 5 个基点⾄ 3.21% ，英国 10 年期国债收益率上⾏ 7 个基点⾄
5.05% 。
美联储主席沃什本周维持利率不变，但有三名官员投票异议，主张⽴即加息以应对持续的通胀
⻛险。
PGIM Credit 全球经济学副主任 Katharine Neiss 表⽰，沃什的新闻发布会表现 " 弱于预期 " ，并预
计美联储的 " 鹰派转向 " 将在 9 ⽉兑现，届时可能连续加息三次。
Brown Brothers Harriman 的 Elias Haddad 指出：
与此同时，据 CME 联邦利率观察⼯具，市场当前对 9 ⽉加息的定价概率为 65% ，较⼀周前的 82%
有所回落。
其他资产：⽇元成关键变量，⽐特币⾛软
美元周五基本持平，过去六个交易⽇已回吐沃什⾸次主持 FOMC 会议后的全部涨幅。
美国经济活动韧性对美元的⽀撑，被沃什未能将强硬通胀表态转化为可信政策⾏动所抵消，令美
联储落后于曲线的⻛险上升。

### Page 9

⽇元周五上涨 0.4% ⾄ 158.82 ，市场揣测⽇本当局或再度⼊市⼲预，叠加⽇本央⾏加快加息节奏
⾄ 9 ⽉的预期，对全球套息交易构成潜在威胁。
⻩⾦周五下跌 1.4% ⾄每盎司 4046.96 美元， 7 ⽉总体原地踏步。
⽐特币下跌 2.8% ⾄ 62930 美元，结束了⽉内⼀度突破 66000 美元的短暂上⾏。

盘、收涨 1.47% 。亚⻢逊收涨 15% ，苹果收跌 7.4% 。美股存储芯⽚指数跌 1.58% ，本周累计跌

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• Is AI a bubble? The extraordinary valuations and scale
of investment raise the question. Missing, though, is the
speculative behaviour of buying an asset now with the
intent of resale for profit later.
• It is natural to want to look for parallels in history. Neither

the late 1990s/early 2000s ‘dot com’ nor the 19th century
railway booms quite match the current episode. Better
analogies may be found in other price cycles stemming from
investment booms, spurred by structural shifts. In the rush
to build for the new technology, construction costs and
end-product prices rise before eventually correcting. Time-
to-build and resource constraints can create price cycles
even where classic speculative behaviour is absent.

• Non-financial goals around geopolitics or a rush to build a
god-like intelligence could blind investors to commercial
reality and encourage investments that do not pay off. To
avoid a mad flap that takes us, Icarus-like, too close to the
sun, choices must be based on cost and ROI.
The exceptional valuations on AI-adjacent firms are matched
only by the extraordinary scale of capital expenditure on
data centres, both overseas and in Australia. The use of

leverage and sometimes opaque financing structures twitch
the antennae of those of us who have been through financial
boom–bust cycles before. Naturally, people question the
return on investment and whether this is a “bubble”. But is it?
It helps to define terms. The usual definition of a bubble is a
deviation of asset prices from “fundamental” value, but this is
only as good as your model of fundamentals. The distinctive
feature in episodes labelled as bubbles is speculative

behaviour: investors buying an asset mainly or solely because
they expect its price will rise so they can resell at a profit.
(In technical terms, the current price of the asset depends
on expectations of future price growth). In the extreme, the
market is in “Greater Fool” territory, where investors admit that
they do not think the revenue warrants the price, but believe
they will find a Greater Fool to buy it later.
Property assets are one of the main areas where “bubbly”

boom–bust cycles can occur, because investors can use
more leverage than for other assets. The asset also does not
become obsolete quickly and much of the value is in the non-
depreciating land or location component.
Data centres are a kind of property, and leverage is clearly
being used to finance their construction. Even so, the
speculative behaviour that typically characterises property
AI-carus?
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
bubbles is missing: nobody is making these investments in

expectation of reselling data centres to make a quick buck. It is
all about the expected value of the computing services these
data centres will provide. Currently the “cost of compute”
seems to be on the rise as the firms building the models
try to shift their customer bases away from free and flat-
rate subscriptions onto higher-cost pay-by-use plans. Yet
everyone knows that AI can only achieve the desired returns
by expanding usage, which means it needs to be cheaper

than the human labour it seeks to replace. Even if investment
decisions are based on expected returns on services provided
by that capital, the realism of those return expectations can
and should be scrutinised.
It is natural to want to look for parallels in history.
The seemingly obvious one is the ‘dot com’ boom of the late
1990s/early 2000s. But while there are some similarities
to that episode, there are also important differences. Back
then, the tech firms building the new technology were not

so financially intertwined with the telco firms buying the
spectrum, laying the fibre – and borrowing the money.
There are also similarities to the railway boom of the 19th
century, except that location is not as inherent to a data centre.
You can train an LLM anywhere you can access the electricity,
water and construction inputs. A railway, by contrast, goes
from somewhere to somewhere else, and the returns to
duplicating the route decline rapidly.

Better analogies to the current AI/data centre boom can be
found in other price cycles. Many boom–bust cycles start
from something real and substantial. A new technology might
instigate an equities boom. Opening up a new region might
instigate a land boom there. The value underlying the boom
remains even after the period of over-exuberance ends. We did
not regret creating the Internet, or building that fibre network,
or opening up regions through better transport.

What we did sometimes do, though, is pay too much for what
we got. If everyone is rushing to build the infrastructure,
hoping not to be the Slower Builder who ends up losing money,
pressure on construction resources rises, and so do construction
prices. We have seen similar cost surges during the mining
“Data centres do not have the same
network effects or location-based
natural monopoly characteristics."

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
boom and more recently in the cycle of infrastructure projects
in the eastern seaboard states. (This is likely one reason why
suppliers of construction materials were so quick to pass
increased fuel and transport costs into their prices.)
Along the way, the cost of the services also rise, precisely

because of the lags involved in expanding the infrastructure.
There is an analogy here in things like shipping costs, which show
boom–bust price cycles even though there has obviously never
been a bubble in shipping services. In shipping, office property
and now data centres, demand might increase but supply is fixed
in the short term because it takes time to build that infrastructure.
The price of the service therefore rises in the upswing phase.

This upswing phase can also spill over into related sectors,
as seen this time in the broader tech sector. Export prices for
Korean semiconductors have exploded in the past 6–9 months,
up more than 150% over the year. Pass-through to US import
prices is more muted, given lags and the usual pricing to market,
but tech inflation has increased. As well as the much-publicised
price increases for iPads and Macs, the CPI components covering
computers and other tech goods have picked up recently in

Australia and other advanced economies, to inflation rates
close to the peak of the pandemic. This is a departure from their
usual deflationary pulse and shows that the current inflationary
environment is about more than the US–Iran conflict.
The high costs of the upswing phase nonetheless contain the
seeds of their reversal. They discourage use of the underlying
service, so adoption is slower than its boosters assume. They
also encourage further innovations to economise on the

expensive input; routing algorithms to choose the most cost-
effective AI model are one example. Over time, as data centres
are completed, the supply of computing power expands and
its price falls. Investments that only made sense when the
service’s price was high will become distressed.
Capitalism has its drawbacks, but at least raw commercial
incentives limit people’s appetite to overpay for things. When
all the focus is instead on geopolitical rivalries or being the first

to build god-like intelligence, commercial discipline is lost. A
potentially beneficial innovation wave instead risks becoming an
Icarus-like mad flap that takes us too close to the sun. That is the
perfect environment for a price cycle, including in the tech sector
more broadly.
The end result need not be a meltdown. That depends on
the debt: how much there is relative to revenues after the
price downswing, who borrowed it and who lent it. The role

of deep-pocketed tech firms eases some concerns. Some
borrowers are substantial firms with other revenues. In
addition, other tech firms who have not leveraged themselves
to the data centre build-out have big enough cash piles to pick
up the pieces (at discounted prices) should some projects come
unstuck. Regulators will be most concerned about whether
the lenders who lent to the hyperscalers and AI firms in turn
funded themselves from parts of the financial system that

amplify shocks through their leverage and connection to the
regulated banking system.
The answer is not to regulate AI out of existence or stymie
its further development – a point the federal government has
clearly heeded in the PM’s latest announcements. The advice
I’d give now is the same as I gave a decade ago, in a different
context: in the face of structural change giving rise to a boom,
go in with your eyes open. Don’t get ahead of yourself. And

have something to catch you when you fall.
Humans have managed to transcend Greek mythology and
master the art of flying without melting. Yet the message of
Icarus’s story endures: there are dangers when hubris goes
unchecked. To avoid getting ahead of yourself, it helps to let
commercial considerations shape decisions, especially around
borrowing. Far better that than basing decisions on imagined
scenarios that do not survive contact with real-world economics.

“What worries me about the current
boom is how far financial considerations
seem to sit in the background"

THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: frayed nerves
0.2%mth, and the prior month's gain was revised lower from
0.4% to 0.1%. The latest readings on consumer demand are
also consistent with inflation returning to target, nominal
control group retail sales up a modest 0.5% in June after a

0.8% gain in May, and the latest Beige Book also pointing
to consumer demand being constrained by cost-of-living
pressures. The duration and severity of the renewed conflict in
the Middle East will be critical to the US growth outlook given
University of Michigan consumer sentiment remains stuck near
multi-decade lows.
Over in Asia, China's Q2 GDP and June activity data provided
a mixed read on their economy. Q2 GDP met expectations at
0.9%qtr, although the annual rate disappointed at 4.3%yr.

Year-to-date, growth remains broadly consistent with our
expectation of 4.7%, but such an outcome would be at the lower
end of authorities' 4.5–5.0% full-year target. The industrial
sector continues to drive aggregate momentum, production
growth rebounding to 5.3%yr in June from around 4.0%yr
through April and May. But the domestic growth story remains
fragile, primarily owing to declining investment, -5.7%ytd in
June. Retail sales growth recovered from -0.6%yr in May to

1.0%yr in June, though that still leaves it at the lower end of the
historical rage. Together, the state of domestic investment and
household consumption call for urgent pro-active stimulus to
support cyclical momentum as well as structural change to help
the gains of trade flow to the wider economy.
The Bank of Korea raised rates by 25bp this week and
signalled further tightening is likely. Inflation remains elevated,
due to a combination of higher energy, exchange rate and food

costs alongside resilient domestic demand. But the BoK's
greater challenge is managing an increasingly two-speed
economy, with tech-related sectors benefiting directly from
the AI and data centre investment boom while others wait
for those gains to spill over. For now, policymakers appear
comfortable leaning against inflation and expecting the
benefits of AI production and investment to broaden; although,
in the interim, this leaves Korea exposed should enthusiasm

around AI turn. Investment and the implications for growth
and policy is a focus of this month's edition of The Wrap on
Asia; and, with a particular focus on AI investment, Chief
Economist Luci Ellis’ weekly essay .
The Westpac–MI Consumer Sentiment Index rose 4.1% in July;
but at 83.9, the index is still in the bottom decile of historical
outcomes, pointing to deep pessimism. Lower fuel prices have
provided some breathing room, contributing to a less-negative

assessment of ‘family finances vs a year ago’ (+5.6%) and ‘family
finances next 12mths’ (+13.4%). Consumers remain sensitive
to developments in the Middle East, however, last week’s re-
escalation of the conflict resulting in a progressive deterioration
in daily responses while the survey was in the field.
Views on the economic outlook are weak and little changed,
the one- and five-years ahead sub-indexes lifting just 0.6%
and 0.7% in the month; more encouragingly, concerns over

the jobs outlook have eased. Most consumers still expect
mortgage rates to rise over the next year, though the share has
declined from 66% to 60%, with a rising proportion reporting
uncertainty about where rates are headed. Ultimately, the
survey reported a reprieve from worst-case fears but is also
evidence of the scale of challenges ahead.
The latest NAB business survey  echoed similar themes.
Business confidence continued to retrace the sharp falls seen

at the onset of the Middle East conflict, rising +9pts to a
less-pessimistic reading of –5. Lower fuel prices contributed
to slower growth in purchase costs and final prices, but the
cumulative impact of these pressures have weighted on capital
expenditure plans and labour demand. Business conditions
remain subdued, holding at +3 for a third consecutive month
having weakened earlier in 2026.
Offshore, US inflation data was supportive of interest rates

remaining on hold in coming months. Headline prices fell 0.4%
in June as energy prices responded to the Middle East ceasefire
agreed mid-month, pulling annual inflation down from 4.2%yr
to 3.5%yr. Of greater significance for monetary policy, however,
was the flat monthly read for core inflation, 2.6%yr.
Members of the FOMC want to see further evidence of
disinflation towards the 2.0%yr target. In our view, the multi-
month trends are consistent with such an out-turn, with

core goods prices broadly unchanged year-to-date and core
services inflation averaging just below 0.3% per month. The
June PPI report corroborates this view, headline producer
prices falling 0.3%, while May was revised down sharply
from 1.1% to 0.6%. Ex food and energy, prices rose a modest

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Soggy activity data ahead of key CPI report
Following last week’s RBNZ meeting, which resulted in
the first OCR hike in three years, the market has turned its
attention to the prospect of a follow-up rate hike at the
next meeting on 2 September. The past week has provided

numerous economic reports and a key RBNZ speech for
the market to digest, covering topics such as business
sentiment, consumer spending, housing, the labour market,
tourism and inflation.
Early in the week, the QSBO – New Zealand’s premier business
survey – pointed to a modest lift in business sentiment in the
June quarter. The survey period ran from 10 June to 7 July. The
average response over that period meant that the headline
confidence index rose 11pts to +12. Early responses were a

net 5% negative, while the second major batch of responses
– mostly arriving after the signing of the Memorandum of
Understanding by the US and Iran – were a net 20% positive.
Given the renewed hostilities in the Middle East this month,
the earlier responses may be more representative of where
sentiment would stand if the survey was re-run today. Firms’
expectations of their own activity, which tend to have a closer
correspondence with GDP growth, were little changed for the

quarter. Other activity indicators were mixed. Investment and
hiring intentions were less negative than in the March quarter,
but profitability was expected to worsen further. Meanwhile,
the rise in fuel prices since March was clearly reflected in the
pricing measures. A net 41% of firms reported raising their
prices in the June quarter, up from 22% last quarter and the
highest reading since September 2023. In the next three
months, a net 54% of firms indicated an intention to raise their

prices – the highest since March 2023.
News from the retail sector was more downbeat than expected
in June, with the value of retail spending processed through the
electronic payments system declining 1.4%, reversing the step
higher seen in May. Of note was a 4% decline in spending on
household durables, while spending on apparel and hospitality
both fell 2%. The only category that saw a rise in June was
spending on groceries. Given the June result, total spending

over the June quarter increased just 0.4%, driven entirely by
a 7.3% lift in spending on fuel. Core retail spending fell 0.1%,
marking the first decline since the March quarter last year.
Our own recent survey of households confirmed that
consumers remain cautious. In response to developments
in the Middle East, 36% of respondents reported having
wound back their discretionary spending and around 15%
have delayed or cancelled travel plans. Even when it comes to

spending on essentials like groceries, around 30% said they’ve
changed how they shop e.g., by purchasing fewer or cheaper
items. Looking ahead, the pressure on fuel costs looks likely to
persist, especially given the rebound in oil prices this week due
to renewed tensions in the Middle East. Combined with other
cost of living pressures, a soft labour market and continued
economic uncertainty, we expect growth in consumer spending
will remain comparatively subdued.

The housing market data also struck a soggy note in June,
with house sales edging lower and so added to a cumulative
9% decline since the start of the year. The median time to sell
was little changed and so remained well above the historic
average. Reflecting buyers’ lack of urgency, the REINZ house
price index fell by 0.3% for the month and was down 0.8% on
Darren Gibbs
Senior Economist

### Page 6

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
a year ago. National average prices have moved sideways for
the last three years, though with some marked distinctions
across regions. Wellington and Auckland have seen the
largest price declines over the last year, while regions with
stronger links to agriculture and tourism, such as Canterbury

and Otago, have seen prices continue to set new highs.
Fixed-term mortgage rates have risen since the start of this
year, in anticipation of the next RBNZ tightening cycle (which
is now in motion). Looking ahead, we expect that higher
interest rates will continue to weigh on the housing market,
leaning against any improvement in incomes as the economy
gets back on its feet. Meanwhile, demand for housing remains
constrained by relatively weak population growth. New

Zealand saw a net inflow of just 1,860 migrants in May, with
a downward revision to earlier months reducing the annual
inflow to less than 19,000. Given historically weak natural
growth in the population due to a declining fertility rate,
annual population growth is running at less than 1%.
In some better news, MBIE’s measure of job advertising picked
up in June, perhaps helped by – at the time – a recovery in
business confidence amidst easing tensions in the Middle East.

The level of advertising remains low by historical standards
but is now 13% higher than a year earlier. Not surprisingly, the
strongest lift in job vacancies was seen in the primary sector,
reflecting buoyant prices for almost all of New Zealand’s key
exported commodities. This is also reflected in the regional
breakdown, with the major urban area of Auckland yet to see
any lift in vacancies from the cycle low point.
Moving on, tourist data for May suggests some impact from the

Middle East conflict with non-resident arrivals declining 2.4%
to be 3.6% below their recent February peak. Even so, arrivals
were around 7% higher than a year earlier reflecting the strong
growth seen since around the middle of last year. Much of that
growth owes to arrivals from Australia – New Zealand’s largest
market – and Asia, including a significant uplift in arrivals from
China (the latter up more than 30% from a year earlier). By
contrast, arrivals from major North American and European

markets were lower than a year earlier. Looking ahead, we
continue to expect a soft patch in tourism arrivals over the
second half of this year due to the impact of high airfares and
Middle East uncertainty on long haul forward bookings.
On balance, today’s Selected Prices data for the June month
– covering around 47% of the CPI – provided a modest upside
surprise. Fuel prices – especially diesel – were lower, as
expected. Food prices posted their expected seasonal increase

while rental inflation remained very subdued. Electricity prices
rose by less than expected, but the very volatile international
accommodation component rose a much stronger-than-
expected 9% during the month. Given this information, we have
nudged up our earlier forecast and now think that next week’s
June quarter CPI report will reveal a 1.5% lift in the headline
index, raising annual inflation to 4.1% from 3.1% at present. We
view the risks around this forecast as balanced. Our estimate of

annual inflation is 0.2ppt above the updated estimated provided
by the RBNZ at the July Monetary Policy Review.
Finally, this week the RBNZ’s Chief Economist, Paul Conway,
gave a speech on pricing behaviour. He emphasised that the key
monetary policy challenge facing policymakers is to prevent
the oil shock from becoming embedded in expectations and
business pricing behaviour. In that regard, he noted several
findings from recent research – some of which is ongoing at the

Bank – which in his view raise the risk of inflation persistence:
• Businesses and households pay much more attention to
inflation when it is above the 1-3% target range, making
expectations more sensitive to current inflation outcomes.
• Household inflation expectations have risen and become
more dispersed since the Middle East conflict, indicating
greater uncertainty about future inflation.
• Firms now change prices more frequently than in the past

due to lower repricing costs and digital technology.
• Businesses are more willing to pass through cost increases
than they are to reverse price rises when costs fall, creating a
greater risk that temporary shocks become lasting inflation.
• These effects appear particularly strong in the services
sector, where inflation tends to be more persistent.
Conway noted that the RBNZ may need to respond more
forcefully to re-anchor expectations if businesses and

households begin to expect inflation to remain elevated.
Balanced against this, he argued – correctly in our view – that
the current situation differs markedly from the inflationary
episodes of the 1970s and from the post-pandemic inflation
surge. In particular, the New Zealand economy currently has
spare capacity, which should make it harder for firms to raise
prices aggressively and should help inflation return to target
over time. Medium-term inflation expectations also remain

relatively well anchored.
Nonetheless, the market interpreted the speech as “hawkish”,
especially when set against the dovish comments that Conway
had previously made in recent months. Combined with
developments in the Middle East and elsewhere, the speech
has consolidated the market’s confidence that the RBNZ will
deliver a further lift in the OCR at the 2 September meeting,
with a 25bp hike now around 90% priced.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Jun Westpac-MI Leading Index (%ann'd)
Jul 22, Last: –0.17
The Leading Index growth rate ticked up slightly to –0.17%
in May, the read still consistent with sluggish, below-trend
growth momentum through the second half of 2026 and into
early 2027.

The June read will include a mixed bunch of component
updates. On the plus side, the ASX200 lifted 0.5% and
consumer sentiment-based measures posted some recovery.
However, there were a significant weakening across several
other components including: commodity prices (–0.7% in
AUD terms); dwelling approvals (–1.1%); total hours worked
(–1.1%); and a further narrowing in the yield spread. That looks
likely to see some further softening in growth momentum

overall.
AUS: Jun Labour Force – Employment Change (000s)
Jul 23, Last: 40.3, Westpac f/c: 15
Market f/c: 15, Range: flat to 50
After expanding around +26k/mth in Q1, employment growth
effectively stalled over April and May. On a three-month
average basis, employment is growing well below the pace
of the working-age population, leaving the employment-to-
population ratio 0.7ppts below its recent historical high.
The brief recovery around the start of the year has well and

truly faded. Higher inflation and recent interest rate rises
will still take time to fully work its way through to the labour
market, but for now, it is looking weaker than earlier in the year.
Forward-looking business survey measures point to sluggish
employment growth ahead. We have pencilled in a +15k lift in
June, and see risks tilted to the downside. See our full preview.
AUS: Jun Labour Force – Unemployment Rate (%)
Jul 23, Last: 4.4, Westpac f/c: 4.4

Market f/c: 4.4, Range: 4.3 to 4.5
The unemployment rate also eased as expected, edging down
from a cycle high 4.5% to 4.4% in May as various sources
of noise faded. Given the participation rate has held up a bit
better than employment, the trend for the unemployment rate
has been a continued grind higher.
For June, we think both the participation and unemployment
rates will hold steady. But as discussed in our preview,
re-published official data and new unofficial measures are

pointing to a material increase in underemployment in recent
months. Both demand-side (weaker employment) and supply-
side (cost-of-living pressures) are likely at play, and it may be
pointing to higher measured unemployment down the track.

### Page 8

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
8
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: Q2 Consumer Price Index (%)
Jul 21, Quarterly, Last: 0.9, Westpac f/c: 1.5
Annual, Last: 3.1%, Westpac f/c: 4.1, RBNZ f/c: 3.9
With the war in the Middle East pushing oil prices sharply
higher, consumer price inflation is set to rise to a two-year

high. We estimate that New Zealand consumer prices rose by
1.5% in the June quarter. The annual inflation rate is expected
to rise to 4.1% (up from 3.1% previously). That’s slightly higher
than the 3.9% the RBNZ assumed in their recent July update,
however much of the surprise is likely related to volatile items
like holiday accommodation. More importantly, core inflation
has been softening but remains above the RBNZ’s 2% target
midpoint. That’s despite the downturn in economic growth and

softness in the labour market.

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 17 July 2026

Charts of the week: Inflation softens

June US CPI data eases immediate inflation worries, but oil remains a risk. China, India equities have room to catch up. 
CPI inflation m/m change vs. 2yr US govt. bond yield    MSCI equity indices: Asia ex-Japan vs. China, India

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered

Editorial 
Inflation cools, but focus shifts back to oil 
Strategy summary: Peak Fed hawkishness is likely behind us 
following the release of a soft June CPI inflation print . This 
reinforces our view that the Fed will keep rates on hold through 
the second half of 2026. However, a new surge in oil prices 
poses some risks to this view. Oil prices are likely to rise to the 
top of our expected USD 70 -90/bbl near -term range, but we  
believe sufficient incentives exist for all sides to avoid a larger 
escalation that pushes prices sustainably above this range. 
We would focus on locking in attractive yields, but retain our 
strong preference for short bond maturities to avoid excessive 
exposure to any new inflation concerns. China and India 
equities should benefit within Asia from any equities broadening 
trade, alongside Taiwan equities. AI monetisation is expected 
to be a key focus for upcoming tech sector earnings.  
US inflation data signals peak hawkishness likely behind 
us: US headline CPI inflation fell -0.4% m/m, the biggest 
monthly drop in years. This supported the view that the rise in 
inflation over Q2 indeed was likely a temporary surge led by oil 
prices alone rather than being a more broad -based risk. The 
data point reinforces our view that the Fed is likely to leave rates 
unchanged through H2 2026 and signals ‘peak hawkishness’ is 
now likely behind us. Fed Chair Warsh’s inclusion of ‘full 
employment’ at his f irst semi -annual testimony to Congress 
supports this view. However, the relief was more visible in 
shorter-term US bond yields (2-year) relative to long-term bond 
yields (10-year), suggesting long term fiscal concerns remain.  
Renewed Middle East escalation poses upside oil price risk 
in the near term:  Following earlier attacks on ships transiting 
through the Strait of Hormuz, the US initiated new military 
strikes on Iran . This series of events is pushing oil prices 
towards the upper end of our expected USD 70 -90/bbl range. 
While there is a non -negligible risk that Trump follows through 
on his threats to escalate further still, we believe the US 
continues to face an incent ive to avoid a larger oil price shock 
given the risk to US growth and upcoming mid -term elections. 
This means any temporary oil price spikes above USD 90/bbl 
are unlikely to be sustained for long. Having said that, we 
continue to see US TIPS (inflation -protected bonds) as an 
attractive hedge against a more adverse scenario. 
Lock in elevated yield:  Fiscal worries notwithstanding, our 
view that we are likely past peak Fed hawkishness means that 
the recent rise in bond yields offers an opportunity to lock in 
yields for income . We maintain a broad preference for credit 
over government bonds, with an  Overweight on EM USD 
government bonds. However, we continue to favour short 
maturities to avoid excessive exposure to volatility triggered by 
any new inflation or fiscal worries. 
China, India equities to benefit from broadening of rally:  
2026 year-to-date gains in Asia ex -Japan equities have been 
driven by Korea and Taiwan equities (ie. semiconductor-heavy 
markets). Chinese and Indian equities have both lagged. 
However, these two markets should benefit from a rotation into 
broader regional exposure and a softening of the US Dollar, 
where long positioning is close to extremes. We remain 
Overweight China and India equ ities, with our preference for 
the Hang Seng tech index providing exposure to a broadening 
of gains within the Asia technology sector. 
Watch upcoming tech sector earnings, UK appointments : 
While semi-conductor sector earnings growth visibility remains 
strong, we also expect solid result announcements from US 
hyperscalers. This is likely to be driven by robust AI adoption 
and digital advertising trends. However, the key market focus is 
likely to remain on the level of AI capex spending and any 
signals on the pace of AI monetarisation.  
In the UK, the key focus for markets following Burnham’s likely 
appointment as Prime Minister will be the choice of Chancellor 
of the Exchequer and any related signals on the extent of fiscal 
policy continuity. These are likely to be key for UK bond yields  
and the GBP, which are expected to stay rangebound till 
greater clarity emerges.  
—  Manpreet Gill

The weekly macro balance sheet

and policy as positive for risk assets in the near-term 
(+) factors:  Easing US price pressures; robust US economic activity 
(-) factors: Escalating geopolitical tensions revive inflation risks

Positive for risk assets Negative for risk assets 
Macro data 
• US headline and core 
consumer and producer

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 17 July 2026

Top client questions 
  What can we learn from major US banks’ Q2 earnings? 
Our view: Major US banks  delivered significant earnings beats 
in Q2 vs. consensus estimates, making them attractive to add 
on pullbacks. We maintain a Core allocation to US financials. 
Rationale: Major US banks beat consensus expectations across 
both earnings and revenues, primarily driven by a surge in equities 
trading and record investment banking fees. Market volatility, AI-
related capital markets activity and mega listings , such as  the 
SpaceX initial public offering,  supported this growth. Positive loan 
growth supported net interest income. Meanwhile, resilient US 
consumers supported the stable credit quality.  
Looking ahead, growth should be supported by a healthy pipeline 
for equity underwriting  and M&A advisory fees . An elevated 
equity market also bolsters fee income for the asset management 
business. Guidance was broadly raised , although management 
flagged tougher H2 comparisons and sounded a cautious tone  
around macro uncertaint ies, inflation risks and big fiscal deficits. 
Valuations of major US banks are elevated but justified, in our view. 
—  Michelle Kam, CFA, Investment Strategist 
  What do you expect from upcoming tech sector earnings, 
including those from US hyperscalers? 
Our view: Earnings growth in the semiconductor s ector 
remains strong with good visibility . We favour diversifying 
exposure into Taiwan equities. We expect US hyperscalers to 
deliver strong growth and prefer gaining exposure through the 
US communication services sector. 
Rationale: Earnings reports from the semiconductor industry point 
to unprecedented long-term visibility. Sustained AI-driven demand 
across both advanced logic and memory chips is giving chipmakers 
the confidence to accelerate capacity expansion plans. Taiwan 
equities’ significant exposure to the semiconductor industry makes 
them a preferred route for gaining exposure to this attractive growth. 
For US hyperscalers, we expect solid results , driven by  cloud 
growth with robust AI adoption, and healthy digital advertising trends. 
The market is likely to focus on whether hyperscalers’ significant AI 
investments are beginning to generate meaningful returns. Hence, 
management commentary on  AI monetisation  – specifically 
regarding new revenue streams through enterprise subscriptions, AI 
model usage growth and consumption-based pricing trends – will be 
critical. We also expect hyperscalers to maintain elevated AI capital 
expenditure ( capex) spending. We see the US communication 
services sector – which is dominated by large internet platforms with 
reasonable valuations – as an attractive opportunistic trade idea. 
—  Fook Hien Yap, Senior Investment Strategist

remains strong

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 17 July 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Does the soft June US consumer inflation print validate 
the view that peak Fed hawkishness is behind us? What is your 
view on US bond yields following the soft inflation print? 
Our view: We believe peak inflation is behind us in the near term 
and expect the Fed to hold rates steady through year-end 2026. 
Short-term yields declined more than long-term yields after the 
soft June US consumer inflation print, validating our view that 
the term premium will remain elevated . We maintain our view 
that the US 2-year Treasury yield will trade in a range of 4.00-
4.25%. While the 10 -year yield has traded above our near -term 
target range of 4.25 -4.50%, we expect it to technically range 
between trend support at 4.47% and resistance at 4.63%. 
Rationale: June US headline and core inflation slowed to 3.5% 
y/y and 2.6% y/y , respectively, coming in below market 
expectations. The data supports our view that peak inflation and Fed 
hawkishness are behind us in the near term and reinforces our view 
that the Fed will remain on hold  for the rest of 2026. Despite the 
renewed Middle East tensions this week, we view the escalation as 
transient and do not expect energy prices to retest their April highs.  
Bond yields fell after the latest US inflation data, with shorter-dated 
US Treasury yields falling more than those at the long end. The 
10-year yield pulled back from its 13 July high of 4.626% to around 
4.59%, while the 2-year yield fell from its one-year high of 4.288% to 
around 4.19%. Markets now expect a less hawkish Fed. However, 
fiscal worries remain and will likely keep the term premium elevated.   
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist 
  What is your  outlook for USD/CHF following the pair ’s 
attempt to recover towards 0.8150? 
Our view: We expect USD/CHF to remain rangebound with a 
bearish bias in the coming weeks, w ith the USD as the key 
driver.  
Rationale: The latest US consumer and producer inflation prints 
were softer than expected , which ha s trimmed Fed rate-hike 
expectations and weighed on the USD. Swiss inflation edged down 
to 0.5% y/y in June from 0.6% y/y . The slowdown reflected a slight 
deceleration in services prices  and some early energy price 
easing.  
We see an opportunity for  price dynamics to gradually 
reaccelerate in the coming quarters as the effect of the Swiss franc’s 
(CHF's) previous appreciation fades. This should allow the Swiss 
National Bank (SNB) to shift its focus towards policy normalisation 
over time. The central bank's increased willingness to intervene 
against excessive CHF strength  remains a latent constraint on 
USD/CHF downside, with SNB President Schlegel having recently 
reaffirmed that stance. The pair rise has been rejected repeatedly at 
0.8150 and is likely to remain rangebound with a bearish bias, with 
support at 0.7920. 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 17 July 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Andy Burnham is widely expected to be confirmed as the 
leader of the UK Labour Party on 17 July . Will this spark 
heightened volatility in the GBP and UK bond (Gilt) yields? 
Our view: We expect Gilt yields and  the GBP to remain 
rangebound in the near term until there is greater clarity on 
Burnham’s policy agenda.  
Rationale: Andy Burnham is expected to become  Prime Minister 
(PM) of the UK on 20 July, having secured the backing of a majority 
of Labour Members of Parliament. The Gilt market is anxious about 
his appointment, as Burnham’s fiscal vision is seen as more left-
leaning than that of outgoing PM Starmer ’s. Using the 10-year Gilt 
as an example, yields have risen by about 30bps since he emerged 
as a strong contender in late June, reflecting these concerns. 
However, Burnham has publicly ruled out changing Chancellor of the 
Exchequer Rachel Reeves’s self -imposed borrowing limits, while 
signalling that he is open to reducing the welfare bill if he becomes 
PM, suggesting some degree of fiscal discipline . That said, the 
market will likely remain concerned about how Burnham may seek 
flexibility within the fiscal rules. He was reportedly considering 
bringing forward a bolstered budget later in 2026 , potentially 
combining it with the Autumn Budget, including an addit ional GBP 
40bn to fund infrastructure and housing investment, alongside the 
removal of defence spending from existing fiscal rules. Perhaps most 
worrying for the market were his comments that the UK had to 
move beyond being “in hock” to bond markets , which 
contributed to the initial yield spike when he entered the PM race. 
Having said that, we think Gilt markets have already embedded a 
meaningful near-term political risk premium. We expect the m to 
remain rangebound for now, as investors await the Autumn Budget 
and Parliament enters its summer recess until September. Whether 
Reeves retains her position in the cabinet  will also be closely 
monitored as a signal of continuity in fiscal policy. 
The larger GBP risk will come after Burnham’s confirmation, 
particularly through his choice of chancellor and the credibility of 
his spending plans . Policies that promote investment and growth 
while respecting fiscal constraints would be broadly neutral to 
positive for the GBP. In contrast, unfunded spending commitments 
or a perceived weakening of fiscal discipline are likely to  revive 
concerns over UK debt sustainability and weigh on the currency. 
Our base case is , therefore, a brief increase in GBP volatility , 
rather than a sustained directional move. GBP/USD is likely to 
remain more sensitive to the US D, US rate expectations , and 
upcoming UK fiscal announcements than to 17 July’s confirmation 
itself.  
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

Gilt yields have already risen ahead of Andy

## Document 343: Sterling’s rally is not built to last

Source PDF: all report pdf/Sterling’s rally is not built to last.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Article | 23 July 2026 1
THINK economic and financial analysis
Article  |  23 July 2026   FX
Sterling’s rally is not built to last
Sterling’s rally has been driven more by positioning, carry and potentially
some M&A flows than by a lasting improvement in UK fundamentals.
With UK short-dated rates likely to drift lower and fiscal risks set to
return ahead of the autumn, we expect sterling to hand back recent
gains. Our central view remains for EUR/GBP to rise towards 0.88 by

year-end
We expect sterling's strength to reverse as we see positioning, carry and M&A flows driving the rally
rather than fundamentals
Not driven by a UK re-rating
Sterling has had a pretty good summer so far. Even though it is off recent highs, sterling has
still managed to rally about 2% against the euro over the last month and has generally
performed well against a strong dollar.
Normally, a sustained rally in sterling would be driven by some good news – be it on growth,

relative rate differentials, fiscal improvements or just capital inflows. It’s hard to pick out
anything concrete here, but justifying sterling’s rally on a perceived fiscal improvement – for
example, Andy Burnham’s choice of chancellor – looks wide of the mark.
Below we show a relationship between the UK risk premium embedded in the gilt market
versus that embedded in the FX market. Through 2025, both 10yr gilt yields and EUR/GBP

### Page 2

THINK economic and financial analysis
Article | 23 July 2026 2
traded above levels normally associated with common financial variables. While that risk
premium has remained in gilts for most of 2026, it has evaporated in sterling. As a result, this
sterling rally is not a classic re-rating story.
Risk premium remains in gilts, not sterling
Source: ING, Refinitiv
Positioning explains more than fundamentals
A better explanation of the sterling rally – one which appeals to us much more – is the

combination of a short squeeze and some one-off flows. On the former, it looked like
speculators had turned exceptionally bearish on sterling ahead of UK local elections in early
May on the (correct) assumption that poor results would spell the end of Prime Minister Keir
Starmer’s premiership. That bearish sterling sentiment can best be seen in the FX options
market, where the risk reversal – the cost of buying a EUR/GBP call over an equivalent put

option – rose to the most expensive levels seen since April 2025. That was when global
financial markets were in turmoil after President Donald Trump’s ‘Liberation Day’ tariffs.
Sterling failed to sell off in the immediate aftermath of those election results, and we suspect a
combination of sterling’s relative high risk-adjusted yields in quiet summer markets and
potentially some large one-off sterling buying flows caught the market exceptionally short. In

terms of those flows, there has been speculation that M&A activity could be in play.

### Page 3

THINK economic and financial analysis
Article | 23 July 2026 3
EUR/GBP versus three month risk reversal
Source: Refinitiv, ING
The M&A story is probably being overstated
Connecting M&A activity with currency moves is typically a thankless task – particularly when
this cross-border activity can be FX-hedged, can be spread over many months and can be
locally funded. Yet the available data does suggest there has been a huge pick-up in UK
targeted M&A flows this year on the view that UK corporates are cheap.

Current price earnings multiples for the FTSE 250 trade at a steep discount to the S&P Euro
index (around 180 names) and announced UK inbound inflows are running at record highs.
Announced flows into the UK dwarf anything seen in continental Europe, with UK deals
focusing on Financials (Schroders, Beazley, Allfunds Group), Consumer Staples (Food
assets/Unilever) and Industrials (Intertek, Rotork).
UK versus eurozone P/E multiples and UK M&A inflows

Source: LSEG, ING calculations
Pending and completed deals for UK companies this year add up to a staggering £220bn on
some measures. But what we would say is that, over the last 25 years, net portfolio flows have
on average been nearly five times larger than net direct investment flows. This can be seen
below when looking at financial account flows as a % of GDP.

### Page 4

THINK economic and financial analysis
Article | 23 July 2026 4
While M&A flows may occasionally grab the headlines in currency markets, they rarely
drive sustained moves in currencies.
UK net investment and portfolio flows versus GBP/EUR
Source: UK Office for National Statistics, Refinitiv
The real driver ahead is the Bank of England
What typically explains currencies is relative growth trends and what they mean for the
monetary and fiscal mix. Coming back to basics, EUR/GBP has lacked a sustained trend since

2022, but interest rate implied fair value has consistently acted as an anchor around 0.86. The
fair-value estimate is derived from a 10-year weekly relationship between EUR/GBP and the
EUR:GBP 2Y swap spread, providing a stable benchmark against which to assess valuation. As
shown in the chart below, periods of substantial over- and undervaluation have typically
reversed over time.
The pair currently sits close to the lower edge of the 1.5-standard-deviation valuation band,

suggesting limited downside potential barring a substantial swing in short-term differentials in
favour of the pound. On the contrary, our swap rate forecasts imply a gradual rise in EUR/GBP
fair value over the coming years. That is based on the house view that UK inflation looks,
barring a much larger energy surge, unlikely to challenge the 4% area – the 4% threshold seen
as the trigger for a Bank of England hike. An unchanged UK policy rate and one further

European Central Bank hike to 2.50% is our house call over the next six to nine months.
Our call on rate spreads suggests EUR/GBP should trade higher from here. But what about the
fiscal story? Could there be any surprises there?

### Page 5

THINK economic and financial analysis
Article | 23 July 2026 5
Short-term rate differential is the medium-term anchor for
EUR/GBP
Source: ING, Refinitiv
Tight fiscal arithmetic becomes harder to ignore
Like much of Europe, there are plenty of reasons to be downbeat about the UK’s public
finances. Spending pressures are growing – from defence to health and social care. Debt
interest costs are high and rising, not helped by Britain’s large stock of index-linked bonds and

increasing reliance on foreign investors (particularly hedge funds). The tax burden may be the
highest in decades, but spending has risen more significantly as a share of GDP since 2019.
And tax on wages – income tax and social security – is among the lowest in Europe as a share
of average labour costs.
That said, the UK is also a rare example of a country undergoing some meaningful fiscal
consolidation. Since 2021, the tax thresholds have been frozen in cash terms. And subsequent

waves of inflation have dragged more and more people into higher tax brackets, increasing tax
revenues as a share of GDP. This fiscal drag is planned to continue over the remainder of this
decade. And it’s a key reason why gilt issuance is falling significantly – from £303bn in FY2025
to £246bn in the current fiscal year.
In short, there’s a more positive near-term public finance story even if longer-term, it’s hard to
see how borrowing doesn’t increase over and above current budget plans.

Over recent weeks, investors had become more relaxed about Burnham’s appointment,
following his commitment to stick to the existing fiscal rules. In theory, that precludes a
stimulus package this autumn that would either materially increase gilt issuance or change
the calculus for the BoE. But Burnham’s recent openness to bigger changes – including lifting
the tax-free allowance and greater funding for social care – means a bolder budget can’t be

ruled out. Investors will be particularly sensitive to any headlines on tweaks to the fiscal rules
in the run-up to Burnham’s first budget this October or November.

### Page 6

THINK economic and financial analysis
Article | 23 July 2026 6
Sterling to hand back gains
Sterling’s summer rally has come as a surprise to most, but it looks to be built on weak
foundations. Into the autumn, we expect UK short-dated rates will be coming lower
and the UK’s fiscal position will again be under scrutiny. That means EUR/GBP doesn’t
need to spend too long down at these levels near 0.85 and we remain comfortable
with our call for a move to 0.88 by year-end and a push to 0.90 in 2027. And based on

## Document 365: “AI股神”是如何崩盘的？华尔街一线复盘爆仓全过程

Source PDF: all report pdf/“AI股神”是如何崩盘的？华尔街一线复盘爆仓全过程.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-07-31
Pages: 13
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

4 倍杠杆、华尔街做空围猎与 1000 亿资产的⼤打劫。
摘要：
AI 算⼒与基础设施交易正在经历⼀场剧烈的⼤挤泡沫。近期，前  OpenAI 员⼯  Leopold
Aschenbrenner 创办的对冲基⾦  Situational Awareness LP （ SALP ） 因⾼杠杆重仓  AI 概念
股及  Anthropic 股权，在市场回调中惨遭主券商强平爆仓（ Liquidation ），引发华尔街巨头竞争
抢购其残余资产。
在最新⼀场播客访谈中，前对冲基⾦经理、知名⾦融评论员  Martin Shkreli 深度拆解了这次爆
仓事件的内幕、华尔街做空围猎的 “ 达尔⽂法则 ” 、⼤体量头⼨平仓的底层机制，以及交易员为何
屡屡倒在杠杆与凯利公式上的深层原因。
华尔街⻅闻整理要点如下：
4 倍杠杆引发剧烈爆仓： SALP 基⾦资产规模达  450 亿美元（含  100 亿  Anthropic 股权），通过
主券商加了  4 倍杠杆，总持仓市值（ GMV ）⾼达  1200 亿美元。在公开市场股票下跌  25% 的打

击下，其净股权迅速缩⽔乃⾄⾯临负数⻛险，被迫触发主券商强平。
华尔街的达尔⽂主义围猎：当市场得知基⾦被迫清算时，多家对冲基⾦采取 “ 对着基⾦打枪
（ Shooting against a fund ） ” 策略 —— 提前卖出同类持仓并⼤⼒做空，加速了其破产进程。
Citadel 与巨头的⾎腥抢购： Citadel 、 Millennium 及  Jane Street 参与了对  SALP 残余资产的闭
⻔竞标。 Citadel 等买家以  20%-50% 的巨额折价接盘其股票簿，获得数⼗亿美元的即时账⾯浮
盈。
AI 基本⾯与边际交易者的背离：尽管  OpenAI 、 Anthropic 及科技巨头业绩强劲，但决定股价短
期⾛势的是那  5% 加了杠杆的边际买卖者，当最脆弱的资⾦恐慌出逃时，任何微⼩的负⾯声⾳
都会引发暴跌。
杠杆与凯利公式的终极警⽰：多数交易者总是严重过度下注（ Overbetting ）。根据凯利公式
（ Kelly Criterion ），即使单笔交易拥有  60% 对  40% 的胜率优势，只要持仓规模超过合理⽐例

（过度下注），在概率⽅差下最终必然⾛向破产归零。
华尔街的达尔⽂法则：传闻落地与做空围猎
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

Martin Shkreli 在访谈中指出，过去  24 ⼩时华尔街经历了⼀场由流动性危机引发的剧烈震荡。
他将本次事件与历史上的⻓期资本管理公司（ LTCM ）及  Amaranth 爆仓案相提并论。
据  Martin 透露，早在周初市场上便已有关于  SALP 基⾦陷⼊困境的传闻。部分对冲基⾦机构敏
锐捕捉到这⼀信号后，迅速执⾏了 “ 对着基⾦打枪（ Shooting against a fund ） ” 的做空策略。在得
知某家机构被迫清算平仓时，市场同业出于弱⾁强⻝的 “ 达尔⽂法则 ” ，会率先抛售与之重合的持
仓，并建⽴⼤量空头头⼨，从⽽加速了该基⾦的破产倒台。
4 倍杠杆拆解： 1000 亿资产如何迅速归零
针对本次  AI 板块的回调原因， Martin Shkreli 强调基本⾯并⾮决定短期股价的核⼼要素，买卖
双⽅的边际意愿才是关键。
他分析称， SALP 基⾦此前管理约  450 亿美元资产（其中约  100 亿美元为  Anthropic 股权），并
在银⾏账⼾保留约  300 亿美元现⾦的前提下，通过主券商获得了⾼达  4 倍的杠杆，使其持仓总

市场价值（ GMV ）飙升⾄  1200 亿美元。当其持有的公开市场股票发⽣约  25% 的回调时，虽然
对  1200 亿基数⽽⾔看似有限，但其本⾦净股权（ Equity ）却从  350 亿骤降⾄  50 亿。主券商
（ Prime Broker ）为了防⽌净股权穿仓引发⾃⾝亏损，会迅速接管投资组合并强⾏平仓。
折价接盘与资产抢购： Citadel 等巨头的套利狂欢
在强平过程中， Jane Street 、 Millennium 以及  Citadel 等华尔街巨头受邀参与了闭⻔竞标。
Martin 表⽰， Citadel 等买家最终以  20% ⾄  50% 的巨额折价接盘了该基⾦的股票资产簿。
据知情⼈⼠透露，接盘⽅在不扰乱市场的前提下处置这些资产，获得了⾼达  30 亿⾄  40 亿美元
的即时账⾯浮盈（ Insta-markup ）。此外， SALP 基⾦经理曾尝试联系多⽅平仓其持有的
Anthropic 私募股权以补充流动性，当时的报价对标全公司约  1.1 万亿美元的估值。 Martin 指

出，这类⼤型危机处置进⼀步巩固了  Citadel 作为华尔街 “ 终极救世主 ” 和影⼦银⾏的地位。
泡沫中的叙事狂热： FOMO 情绪与基本⾯脱节
Martin Shkreli 回顾了历次⾦融泡沫的演变规律，指出从  2000 年互联⽹泡沫到⽊头姐（ Cathie
Wood ）的创新基⾦，每⼀代市场周期中都会出现因过度信仰某种宏⼤叙事（如  AGI 的到来）⽽
押上全部⾝家的交易员。
他指出，尽管  OpenAI 与  Anthropic 等头部  AI 公司的业绩表现强劲，科技巨头的资本⽀出也维
持⾼位，但当市场被  5% 加了⾼杠杆的边际买家所主导时，泡沫最顶部的脆弱资⾦（ Weakest
hands ）会率先恐慌出逃。此时任何微⼩的负⾯信号 —— 例如某家巨头宣布适度缩减资本⽀出，
都会导致整个交易结构轰然塌陷。
⼤体量清算机制：流动性陷阱下的解盘困境
⾯对 “ 为何⼤机构不能直接在⼆级市场平仓 ” 的疑问， Martin 详细拆解了⼤体量头⼨的处置流程。
他解释道，直接向盘⼝抛售（ Selling into the screens ）极易暴露意图并引发量化算法与同业机

构的抢跑做空（ Front-running ）。因此，主券商通常会通过四位数做市商代码（如⾼盛的
GSCO ）进⾏隐蔽询价，或直接将资产簿打包进⾏⼤甩卖。在主券商强⾏控盘的情况下，清算机
构的⽬标是迅速切断⻛险，⽽⾮等待市场反弹，这种⼤体量抛售给市场带来了持续数周的剧烈
下⾏压⼒。
杠杆魔咒与凯利公式：过度下注的必然破产

### Page 3

在探讨基⾦经理的交易⼼态时， Martin 引⽤了⻉尔实验室学者提出的 “ 凯利公式 ” （ Kelly
Criterion ）。
他指出，⼤部分交易员普遍存在严重的过度下注（ Overbetting ）倾向，其持仓规模往往达到合
理建仓标准的  2 到  10 倍。模拟数据显⽰，即便某笔交易拥有  60% 对  40% 的胜率优势，⼀旦
违背凯利公式进⾏过度下注，在⻓期的⽅差波动下，最终结果必然是资⾦归零。 Martin 总结
称，⽆论交易员的个⼈才华多么出众，⼀旦⽆视仓位管理与杠杆⻛险，市场终将教会每⼀个⼈
保持谦逊。
以下为访谈全⽂：

是的，这会尽可能快地加速其倒台。当这种事情发⽣时，这是⼀种⾮常常⻅的操作。嗯，当然，
我并不是说我与他们有重叠的头⼨，所以我肯定不会这么做；但我知道有⼤量的基⾦在做空所有
